is constant and
\tau={\ell}s
. In this case, the solutions for
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
are given in terms of initial conditions and
\tau
(or
{\ell}s
) by

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{cc}{\text{\footnotesize{for any central }}}\\
{\text{\footnotesize{force motion}}}\end{array}\left\{\quad\begin{array}[]{llll}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{\tau}\,=\,{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\cos\tau\,+\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\sin\tau\\[6.0pt]
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{\tau}\,=\,-{\ell}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\sin\tau\,+\,{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\cos\tau\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle(\tau={\ell}s)

eq.(
1.1.4
) -
1.1.4
hold for all arbitrary central forces.

Radial Motion.

The dynamics for
u
and
p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}
may be linearized if we choose
n=-1
such that
u=\tfrac{1}{r}
and
p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}=-r^{2}\dot{r}=-\tfrac{\mathrm{d}r}{\mathrm{d}s}
, but only if
V^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}(r)
is some linear combination of
r^{-1}
and
r^{-2}
.
Specifically,

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rllllll}{\text{if: }}&\left\{\begin{array}[]{lllll}&n=-1\;\;\Rightarrow\;\;\begin{array}[]{lll}&u=\tfrac{1}{r}\\
&p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}=-r^{2}\dot{r}=-\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}r\end{array}&\hskip 18.49988pt{\text{and}}&\hskip 18.49988ptV^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\,=\,-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.16998pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{1}}{r}\,-\,\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.16998pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{2}}{2r^{2}}\,\equiv\,-{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.09999pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{1}u\,-\,\tfrac{1}{2}{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.09999pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{2}u^{2}\end{array}\right.\\[12.0pt]
{\text{then: }}&\left\{\begin{array}[]{lllll}&\tfrac{\mathrm{d}^{2}{u}}{\mathrm{d}{s}^{2}}+\omega^{2}u-{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.09999pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{1}\;=\,f_{u}&=-\tfrac{1}{u^{2}}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\[8.0pt]
&\tfrac{\mathrm{d}^{2}{u}}{\mathrm{d}{\tau}^{2}}+\varpi^{2}u-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.16998pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}\;=\tfrac{1}{{\ell}^{2}}(f_{u}-\tfrac{p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}}{{\ell}}{\vphantom{f}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{f}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})&=-\tfrac{1}{{\ell}^{2}u^{2}}\big({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}+\tfrac{up_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}}{{\ell}}{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\big){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\end{array}\right.\end{array}

where
{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{1},{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{2}\in{\mathbb{R}}
are any scalar constants, and where
\omega^{2}={\ell}^{2}-{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{2}
and
\varpi^{2}=\tfrac{\omega^{2}}{{\ell}^{2}}
are the natural frequency for
s
and
\tau
, respectively.
For unperturbed motion (
{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=0
), the right-hand-side of the above second-order equations for
u
vanish, and
{\ell}
is constant, as are both natural frequencies with
\omega s=\varpi\tau
(since
\tau={\ell}s
for all central force dynamics). If
{\ell}^{2}-{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{2}>0
, the above with
{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=0
describe driven harmonic motion with solution

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rlll}u_{\tau}&\,=\,(u_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{1}}{\omega^{2}})\cos\varpi\tau\,+\,\tfrac{1}{\omega}w_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\sin{\varpi\tau}\,+\,\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{1}}{\omega^{2}}\\[6.0pt]
w_{\tau}&\,=\,-{\ell}(u_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{1}}{\omega^{2}})\sin\varpi\tau\,+\,w_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\cos\varpi\tau&\quad,\quad p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}=\tfrac{w}{u^{2}}\end{array}

where, for convenience, we have defined a new momenta-level coordinate,
w
, as
13
13

13

Some expressions are simply more compact when expressed in terms of
w
rather than
p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}
. The set
({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},u,{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},w)
is
not
canonical.

\displaystyle w\,:=\,u^{2}p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}\,=\,\tfrac{\mathrm{d}u}{\mathrm{d}s}\,=\,{\ell}\tfrac{\mathrm{d}u}{\mathrm{d}\tau}\,=\,-\tfrac{\mathrm{d}r}{\mathrm{d}t}

\displaystyle\leftrightarrow

\displaystyle p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}\,=\,\tfrac{w}{u^{2}}\,=\,r^{2}w\,=\,-\tfrac{\mathrm{d}r}{\mathrm{d}s}

(116)

1.1.5  Kepler Solution

For the remainder of this summary, we will limit consideration to the case that
n=-1
(
u=\tfrac{1}{r}
) and that

V^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}
is the Kepler potential such that
{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{2}=0
,
{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{1}=G(m_{a}+m_{b})=:{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}>0
,
\omega={\ell}^{2}
, and
\varpi=1
in eq.(
1.1.4
).
The second order equations for
u=\tfrac{1}{r}
then become

\displaystyle n\,=\,-1\quad,\qquad V^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\,=\,-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{r}\;\equiv\;-{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}u

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lllll}\tfrac{\mathrm{d}^{2}{u}}{\mathrm{d}{s}^{2}}+{\ell}^{2}u-{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}\;=\,f_{u}\\[5.0pt]
\tfrac{\mathrm{d}^{2}{u}}{\mathrm{d}{\tau}^{2}}+u-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}}\;=\tfrac{1}{{\ell}^{2}}(f_{u}-\tfrac{p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}}{{\ell}}{\vphantom{f}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{f}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})\end{array}

for unperturbed motion, the right hand side vanishes and
{\ell}
is constant such that
\tau={\ell}s
. Either of the above may then be solved to obtain the solution

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{cc}{\text{\footnotesize{for Kepler }}}\\
{\text{\footnotesize{motion}}}\end{array}\quad\left\{\;\;\begin{array}[]{llll}u_{\tau}\;=\,(u_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}})\cos\tau\,+\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}w_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\sin{\tau}\,+\,\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}}\\[5.0pt]
w_{\tau}\;=\,-{\ell}(u_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}})\sin\tau\,+\,w_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\cos\tau&\quad,\quad p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}=\tfrac{w}{u^{2}}\end{array}\right.

The solution for
p_{u,\tau}
is easily obtained from
u_{\tau}
and
w(\tau
).

Cartesian Position & Velocity Coordinates.

The transformation
({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})\mapsto({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})
given in eq.(
1.1.2
), for the case
m=n=-1
and simplified using the integrals of motion
q=1
and
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=0
, reduces to

\displaystyle{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})\;=\;\tfrac{1}{u}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}

,

\displaystyle{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})\;=\;u{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,-\,u^{2}p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;\equiv\;u{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,-\,w{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}

(133)

The Kepler solutions for the cartesian position and velocity components in the inertial basis,
({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})
, are then recovered using eq.(
133
), leading to

14
14

14

The relations seen in eq.(
1.1.2
) may also be used (with
n=-1
) to obtain
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{\tau}
and
{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{\tau}
.

\displaystyle{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{\tau}\;=\;\dfrac{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\cos\tau\,+\,{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\sin\tau}{(u_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}})\cos\tau\,+\,w_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\sin{\tau}\,+\,\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}}}

,

\displaystyle{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{\tau}\;=\;{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}+\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}}({\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{\tau}-{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}})

where
{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=u_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}-w_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}
.

1.1.6  New Orbital Elements for the Perturbed Two-Body Problem

The initial conditions of the Kepler solutions for
({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},u,{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},w)
are used to define a set of eight orbital elements for the arbitrarily-perturbed two-body problem. We use the initial conditions of
w=u^{2}p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}
, defined in eq.(
116
), rather than the initial conditions of
p_{\scalebox{0.7}{$u$}}
itself. The algebraic relation between the fast variables,
({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},u,{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},w)
, and the eight orbit elements,
({\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},U,{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},W)
— which correspond to
({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}},u_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}},{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}},w_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}})
— is a
\tau
-dependent transformation given by (still using
n=-1
):

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lll}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,=\,{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\cos\tau+\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\sin\tau\\[6.0pt]
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,=\,-{\ell}{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\sin\tau+{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\cos\tau\\[6.0pt]
u\,=\,(U-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.16998pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}})\cos\tau+\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}W\sin{\tau}+\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.16998pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}}\\[6.0pt]
w\,=\,-{\ell}(U-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.16998pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}})\sin\tau+W\cos\tau\end{array}

\displaystyle\;\;\leftrightarrow\;\;

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lll}{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,=\,{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\cos\tau-\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\sin\tau\\[6.0pt]
{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,=\,{\ell}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\sin\tau+{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\cos\tau\\[6.0pt]
U\,=\,(u-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.16998pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}})\cos\tau-\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}w\sin\tau+\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.16998pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}}\\[6.0pt]
W\,=\,{\ell}(u-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.16998pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}})\sin\tau+w\cos\tau\end{array}

where
{\ell}=p=P
. For pure Keplerian motion,
(\;)_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}:=\left.{(\;)}\right|_{\tau=0}
are simply initial conditions (constants) at
\tau=0
.
However, we now consider the perturbed problem the above
({\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},U,{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},W)
are osculating elements rather than initial conditions.
Note that
\tau=0
may be chosen arbitrarily but, if it is chosen to correspond to periapse then
{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
define the osculating perifocal basis in precisely the same way that
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
define the osculating LVLH basis. That is, for both perturbed and unperturbed motion, we have
15
15

15

All but the last statement in eq.(
1.1.6
) still hold if
\tau=0
corresponds to some point other than pariapse. The triad
\{{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\hat{{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\hat{{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\}
simply defines the “osculating initial LVLH basis”.

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lll}q^{2}\,=\,Q^{2}=1\\[4.0pt]
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,=\,{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,=\,0\end{array}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lll}p^{2}\,=\,P^{2}\,=\,{\ell}^{2}\\[4.0pt]
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\times{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,=\,{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\times{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,=\,{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\end{array}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lll}&\{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\hat{{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\}\,={\text{\footnotesize{ osculating LVLH basis}}}\\[4.0pt]
&\{{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\hat{{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\hat{{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\}\,={\text{\footnotesize{ osculating perifocal basis}}}\end{array}

In the presence of non-Keplerian perturbations, the governing equations for the osculating elements
({\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},U,{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},W)
are found using variation of parameters; the relations on the left of eq.(
1.1.6
) are differentiated with respect to
\tau
, but with the “initial conditions” treated as unknown functions of
\tau
rather than constants. The resulting expressions are then required to satisfy the perturbed equations of motion which are found from eq.(
1.1.4
), eq.(
84
), and eq.(
116
) as

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{p}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}

,

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}\,=\,-p{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;+\;\tfrac{1}{{\ell}u^{2}}{\vphantom{f}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{f}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}

,

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}u}{\mathrm{d}\tau}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{p}w

,

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}w}{\mathrm{d}\tau}\,=\,-pu+\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.16998pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{p}\;+\;\tfrac{1}{p}f_{u}

Solving the resulting expression for
\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}({\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},U,{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},W)
leads to

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{llll}\tfrac{\mathrm{d}{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}\;=\;-\tfrac{\sin\tau}{{\ell}^{2}u^{2}}({\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}-{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\otimes{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{f}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{f}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\[6.0pt]
\tfrac{\mathrm{d}{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}\;=\;\tfrac{\cos\tau}{{\ell}u^{2}}({\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}-{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\otimes{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{f}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{f}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,+\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}u^{2}}({\hat{{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\otimes{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{f}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{f}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\[6.0pt]
\tfrac{\mathrm{d}u}{\mathrm{d}\tau}\;=\;-\tfrac{1}{{\ell}^{2}u^{2}}\big(u^{2}f_{u}\sin\tau\,-\,\big[\tfrac{w}{{\ell}}\sin\tau-\tfrac{2{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.16998pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}}(1-\cos\tau)\big]{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{f}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{f}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\big)\\[6.0pt]
\tfrac{\mathrm{d}W}{\mathrm{d}\tau}\;=\;\tfrac{1}{{\ell}u^{2}}\big(u^{2}f_{u}\cos\tau-\big[\tfrac{w}{{\ell}}\cos\tau-(\tfrac{W}{{\ell}}\,+\,\tfrac{2{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.16998pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}}\sin\tau)\big]{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{f}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{f}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\big)\end{array}

where
{\vphantom{f}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{f}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=-\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}+{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
f_{u}=-\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial u}+\alpha_{u}
are the perturbing terms, given in terms of
{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=-\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}+{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}
(inertial cartesian coordinate vector of total non-Keplerian perturbing force per unit mass) by eq.(
62
). Thus, the right-hand-side of the above are expressed in terms of
{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
as

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{llll}\tfrac{\mathrm{d}{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}\;=\;-\tfrac{1}{{\ell}^{2}u^{3}}\sin\tau({\hat{{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\otimes{\hat{{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\[6.0pt]
\tfrac{\mathrm{d}{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}\;=\;\tfrac{1}{{\ell}u^{3}}(\cos\tau\,{\hat{{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\otimes{\hat{{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\,+\,{\hat{{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\otimes{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\[6.0pt]
\tfrac{\mathrm{d}U}{\mathrm{d}\tau}\;=\;\tfrac{1}{{\ell}^{2}u^{3}}\big((u{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}+\tfrac{w}{{\ell}}{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})\sin\tau\,-\,\tfrac{2{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.16998pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}}(1-\cos\tau){\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\big){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\[6.0pt]
\tfrac{\mathrm{d}W}{\mathrm{d}\tau}\;=\;-\tfrac{1}{{\ell}u^{3}}\big((u{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}+\tfrac{w}{{\ell}}{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})\cos\tau\,-\,(\tfrac{W}{{\ell}}+\tfrac{2{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.16998pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}}\sin\tau){\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\big){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\end{array}

where
{\hat{{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}={\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\times{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}={\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\times{\hat{{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
with
{\ell}^{2}=p^{2}=P^{2}
.
The above dynamics may be expressed using
s
or
t
as the evolution parameter by use of
\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}={\ell}\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}
or
\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}=u^{2}{\ell}\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}
. Using any of these evolution parameters, the above equations of motion are singularity free except for the case of rectilinear motion (
{\ell}=0
).

The dynamics for
W
are more easily expressed than those of
P_{\scriptscriptstyle{U}}
. However, The equation for
P_{\scriptscriptstyle{U}}
itself may be found using
P_{\scriptscriptstyle{U}}=\tfrac{W}{U^{2}}
.

2.  PROJECTIVE COORDINATES FOR HAMILTONIAN DYNAMICS IN EUCLIDEAN SPACE

We will formulate Hamiltonian particle dynamics in Euclidean space in terms of a canonical extension of the projective decomposition (rather, a family of such decompositions).
First, we must address the general problem of constructing a canonical transformation that is induced by a point transformation from minimal to redundant coordinates.

2.1  Canonical Point Transformations with Redundant Coordinates

Consider
†
†

Some of the content of this chapter has appeared previously in conference papers by myself (Peterson) , Majji, and Junkins
[
28
,
31
]
. It is published here with their permission.

a point transformation from some minimal set of
n
generalized coordinates,
{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
, to some new set of
n+m
(for
m\geq 1
)
non-minimal
coordinates,
{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
:
16
16

16

Later, for the 2BP (
n=3
), we will be interested in using four non-minimal coordinates such that
m=1
. However, for the sake of generality, this section will develop the case for arbitrary
m\geq 1
.

\displaystyle{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;=\;{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)

\displaystyle{\text{\footnotesize{with }}}\;\;\begin{array}[]{ll}&{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\\[3.0pt]
&\;{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n+m}\end{array}

Since
{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
are over-parameterized by
m
degrees, there will be
m
constraint functions,
{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m}
— which we shall take to be holonomic and rheonomic — of the new coordinates such that

\displaystyle{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)\;=\;{\vphantom{0}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{0}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\hskip 18.49988pt,\hskip 18.49988pt\text{d}{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;=\;\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\text{d}{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\;+\;\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial t}{\mathrm{d}}t\;=\;{\vphantom{0}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{0}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}&({\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m})\end{array}

We wish to find the canonical point transformation induced by the point transformation of eq.(
2.1
), subject to the above constraints. That is, the general problem we wish to solve is as follows:

\,\scriptscriptstyle{\blacksquare}\,

Suppose we are given the Hamiltonian
{{K}}({\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},t)
for known canonical coordinates
{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
and
{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
. If we then specify a point transformation,
{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}={\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)
— for non-minimal coordinates
{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n+m}
subject to
m
constraints
{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m}
— what then is the momenta transformation,
{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}={\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)
, and the new Hamiltonian,
{{H}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)
, such that the non-minimal coordinates,
({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2(n+m)}
, are canonical (in the sense they obey Hamilton’s equations for
{{H}}
)?

There are several approaches one may take to answer this question. We will start with Hamilton’s Principle which says that, out of all possible paths between two points, the actual path taken is the one for which the action is stationary:
17
17

17

In the case that non-conservative forces are present, the work from these forces would also be added to the integrand. See section
A
.

\displaystyle\delta I\,=\,\delta\int_{t_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}}^{t_{f}}{{L}}\,{\mathrm{d}}t\,=\,\delta\int_{t_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}}^{t_{f}}({\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\dot{{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}-{{K}})\,{\mathrm{d}}t\,=\,0

(179)

For any canonical transformation, the relation between the original and new phase spaces can be found from the condition that Hamilton’s principle in the above form
must hold in both phase spaces:

\displaystyle\delta I\;=\;\delta\int_{t_{o}}^{t_{f}}({\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\dot{{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}-{{K}})\,{\text{d}}t\;\;=\;\;0\;\;=\;\;\delta\int_{t_{o}}^{t_{f}}({\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\dot{{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}-{{H}})\,{\text{d}}t

(180)

The above will certainly hold if the integrands are equal:
18
18

18

Note that this is
not
the most general relation between the two phase spaces for a canonical transformation. Hamilton’s principle still holds if the time-derivative of some arbitrary generating function is added to either side and/or if either side is multiplied by some scalar constant
[
32
]
. Such an approach leads to a more general family of conical transformations. However, we are only concerned with the canonical point transformation following from some specified
{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)
. For this, the generating function approach is unnecessary.

\displaystyle{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\text{d}{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;-\;{{K}}{\text{d}}t\;=\;{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\text{d}{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\;-\;{{H}}{\text{d}}t

(181)

However, because the coordinates are non-minimal, the
\text{d}{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
are not independent but, rather, are related through the
m
constraints in eq.(
2.1
). We can include these
m
constraints in the above expression by adding an additional term,
{\vphantom{\lambdaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\lambdaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\text{d}{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
— where
{\vphantom{\lambdaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\lambdaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m}
are Lagrange multipliers — to the right-hand-side of the above, leading to the differential relation

\displaystyle{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\text{d}{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;-\;{{K}}{\text{d}}t\;=\;{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\text{d}{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\;-\;{{H}}{\text{d}}t\;-\;{\vphantom{\lambdaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\lambdaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\text{d}{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}

(182)

Since
\text{d}{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}={\vphantom{0}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{0}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
, the above relation still ensures that Hamilton’s principle, as given by equation eq.(
180
), holds in both phase spaces. Then, using the relations
{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}={\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)
and
{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}={\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)
— which are presumed to be specified — to express
\text{d}{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
\text{d}{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
in terms of
\text{d}{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
and
{\text{d}}t
, the above leads to

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}&\hskip 9.24994pt{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}(\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\text{d}{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\;+\;\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial t}{\text{d}}t)\;-\;{{K}}{\text{d}}t\;\;=\;\;{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\text{d}{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\;-\;{{H}}{\text{d}}t\;-\;\lambda{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}(\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\text{d}{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\;+\;\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial t}{\text{d}}t)\\[8.0pt]
\Rightarrow&\hskip 9.24994pt({\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}-\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;-\;\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{\lambdaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\lambdaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\text{d}{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\;-\;({{H}}-{{K}}\;+\;{\vphantom{\lambdaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\lambdaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial t}\;+\;{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial t}){\text{d}}t\;=\;0\end{array}

With the Lagrange multiplier allowing the differentials to be treated as independent, the above condition requires that the coefficients of
\text{d}{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
and
{\mathrm{d}}t
must be zero.
Setting the coefficient of
\text{d}{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
to zero leads to

\displaystyle{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\;=\;\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;+\;\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{\lambdaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\lambdaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}

(185)

The above gives us the definition of the non-minimal momenta,
{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
. This may be conveniently combined into a single matrix equation:

\displaystyle{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\;=\;B^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}\begin{pmatrix}{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\
{\vphantom{\lambdaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\lambdaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\end{pmatrix}\hskip 9.24994pt,

\displaystyle B({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)\;:=\;\begin{pmatrix}\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\\[5.0pt]
\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\end{pmatrix}

(186)

where
B\in{\mathbb{R}}^{(n+m)\times(n+m)}

is given by the Jacobians of eq.(
2.1
) and eq.(
2.1
) with respect to
{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
. Thus, the above gives
{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
as a function of the form
\,{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{\lambdaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\lambdaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},t)
. In order to obtain an expression for
\,{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}({\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{\lambdaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\lambdaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},t)
,
eq.(
2.1
) and eq.(
2.1
) must be used in order to re-express
B
in terms of
{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
rather than
{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
.
However, in order to obtain the desired momenta transformation,
{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)
, we leave
B
as a function of
{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
. Then, so long as
B
is non-singular, the above may be inverted to find
{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{\lambdaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\lambdaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
in terms of the non-minimal coordinates:

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)\\[3.0pt]
{\vphantom{\lambdaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\lambdaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)\end{pmatrix}\;\;=\;\;B^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{--T}}}}}{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}

(187)

Lastly, setting the coefficient of
{\text{d}}t
equal to zero in eq.(
2.1
) leads to the relation between the Hamiltonian for the non-minimal phase space,
{{H}}
, and the Hamiltonian for the minimal phase-space,
{{K}}
:

\displaystyle{{H}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)\;=\;{{K}}\,-\,{\vphantom{\lambdaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\lambdaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial t}\,-\,{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial t}

(188)

We have now solved the problem we set out to solve in this section; given the Hamiltonian
{{K}}({\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},t)
for known minimal canonical coordinates
{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
, along with a specified point transformation
{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)
, then the momenta transformation,
{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)
, is given by eq.(
187
) and the non-minimal Hamiltonian,
{{H}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)
, is given by eq.(
188
). When forming the new Hamiltonian,
{{H}}
, the right-hand-side of eq.(
188
) must be expressed in terms of
{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
and
{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
. This may be done by substituting the point transformation,
{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)
, and constraints,
{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)
— both of which are assumed to be given — along with the momenta transformation,
{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)
, into the right-hand-side of eq.(
188
).

•

Generating Function.

Although this work makes little use of generating functions, readers familiar with this approach to canonical transformations may care to note that the above developments are also obtained by using a "type-2" generating function given by

\displaystyle G_{2}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},t)\,=\,{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,+\,{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{\lambdaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\lambdaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}

(189)

where
{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
are expressed as functions of
{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
and
t
using some specified point transformation and constraint as in eq.(
2.1
) and eq.(
2.1
). One then has the usual relations
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=\tfrac{\uppartial G_{2}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}
and
{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=\tfrac{\uppartial G_{2}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}
.

•

Partitioning of the Non-Minimal Coordinates.

The non-minimal phase space coordinates are over-parameterized by some integer number
m\geq 1
such that
{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n+m}
and
{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n+m}
, where
n
is the degrees of freedom of the system (minimal number of coordinates needed).
Although not necessary, it can be helpful to partition
{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
and
{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
into
n
-dimensional and
m
-dimensional parts:

\displaystyle{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\;=\;({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{u}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{u}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})\hskip 9.24994pt,

\displaystyle{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\;=\;({\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{u})

(190)

such that the dimensions of the coordinate vectors in the above are as follows:

\displaystyle\left.\begin{array}[]{lcl}{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}&,&{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\[2.0pt]
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}&,&{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\end{array}\;\right\}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}

\displaystyle\left.\begin{array}[]{lcl}{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}&,&{\vphantom{\lambdaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\lambdaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\[2.0pt]
{\vphantom{u}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{u}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}&,&{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{u}\end{array}\;\right\}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m}

\displaystyle\left.\begin{array}[]{ll}{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\\[2.0pt]
{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\end{array}\;\right\}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n+m}

eq.(
185
) is then written as

\displaystyle{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;=\;\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;+\;\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{\lambdaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\lambdaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;\;,

\displaystyle{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{u}\;=\;\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{u}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{u}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;+\;\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{u}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{u}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{\lambdaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\lambdaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}

(197)

which leads to eq.(
186
) and eq.(
187
) written as

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{u}\end{pmatrix}\;=\;B^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}\begin{pmatrix}{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\
{\vphantom{\lambdaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\lambdaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\end{pmatrix}\hskip 9.24994pt,

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\
{\vphantom{\lambdaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\lambdaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\end{pmatrix}\;=\;B^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{--T}}}}}\begin{pmatrix}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{u}\end{pmatrix}\hskip 9.24994pt,

\displaystyle B({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)\,:=\,\begin{pmatrix}\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}&\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{u}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{u}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\\[5.0pt]
\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}&\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{u}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{u}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\end{pmatrix}

(198)

With this partitioning,
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
contain the minimal number of coordinates and their corresponding momenta, respectively, while
{\vphantom{u}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{u}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{u}
contain
m
“extra” coordinates and their corresponding momenta. In this formulation, we have defined
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
to have the minimal
number
of coordinates, but these
n
coordinates need not constitute a set of minimal coordinates themselves. For example, for the 2BP (
n=3
) the projective decomposition uses
3+1
coordinates,
{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}=({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},u)
, with
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}={\hat{{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
being three coordinates for the components of the radial unit vector (one of which is redundant) and
u
being a power of the radial distance. In section
2.2
we will use similar coordinates and apply the proceeding developments to obtain their
3+1
conjugate momenta,
{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}=({\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},p_{u})
, and the corresponding Hamiltonian.

An Alternative Derivation for Time-Independent Transformations and Constraints.

We now offer an alternative derivation of the above developments which applies for the particular case that the point transformation and constraint functions are not explicitly dependent on the time,
t
. This derivation is analogous to that of Ferrandiz
[
23
]
.
Starting with a given time-independent point transformation and time-independent constraint

\displaystyle{\text{\footnotesize{point transformation:}}}\hskip 9.24994pt{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;=\;{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})

\displaystyle{\text{\footnotesize{subject to:}}}\hskip 9.24994pt{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})\;=\;{\vphantom{0}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{0}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}

(199)

Unlike before, we do no seek to derive a relation between
{{H}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
and
{{K}}({\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})
. Instead, we require that
{{H}}
is formed by direct substitution of the point transformation and an appropriately defined momenta transformation:
19
19

19

As it would be in the case of a standard time-independent canonical transformation between two sets of
2n
canonical coordinates.

\displaystyle{{H}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})\;=\;{{K}}\big({\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}),{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})\big)

(200)

Our goal then is to find the momenta transformation,
{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
, such that the Hamiltonian formed as above will produce the correct equations of motion for
{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
and
{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
(via Hamilton’s canonical equations).
We begin by noting that
{\dot{{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
and
\dot{{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}
can be combined into a single matrix equation as

\displaystyle\left.\begin{array}[]{ll}{\dot{{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\dot{{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\\[5.0pt]
\dot{{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial\varphi}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\dot{{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\,=\,{\vphantom{0}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{0}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\end{array}\right\}\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\Rightarrow\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\begin{pmatrix}{\dot{{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\\[3.0pt]
{\vphantom{0}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{0}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\end{pmatrix}\,=\,\begin{pmatrix}\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\\[3.0pt]
\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\end{pmatrix}\dot{{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\,=\,B\dot{{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\,=\,B\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}

Where, by definition, we have required that
\dot{{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}=\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}
and where the above matrix,
B({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
, is the same as that defined in eq.(
187
).
From eq.(
200
), and the fact that
{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
is not a function of
{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
, we see that

\displaystyle\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\;=\;\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}\tfrac{\uppartial{{K}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\;=\;\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\dot{{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}

(203)

where we have used
{\dot{{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}=\tfrac{\uppartial{{K}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}
for the second equality.
eq.(
2.1
) then leads to

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}{\dot{{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\\[3.0pt]
{\vphantom{0}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{0}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\end{pmatrix}\;=\;B\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\dot{{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle B\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}\;=\;\begin{pmatrix}{\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{\scriptscriptstyle{{n\times n}}}\\[5.0pt]
0_{\scriptscriptstyle{{m\times n}}}\end{pmatrix}

(204)

We are interested in finding the momenta transformation,
{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
, for which the above holds. If
{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
were of equal dimension then the right-hand-side would simply be a square identity matrix and we could define the momenta relation as
\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}=B^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{--T}}}}}
. Instead, we have
{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
and
{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n+m}
.  As such, it will now be helpful to use the partitioning of eq.(
190
) such that
{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}=({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{u}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{u}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})
and
{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}=({\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{u})
, with
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
and
{\vphantom{u}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{u}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{u}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m}
. The above condition is then written as

\displaystyle B\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}\;=\;\begin{pmatrix}\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}&\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{u}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{u}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\\[5.0pt]
\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}&\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{u}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{u}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}\\[5.0pt]
\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{u}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}\end{pmatrix}\;=\;\begin{pmatrix}\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}+\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{u}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{u}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{u}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}\\[5.0pt]
\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}+\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{u}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{u}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{u}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}\end{pmatrix}\;=\;\begin{pmatrix}{\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{\scriptscriptstyle{{n\times n}}}\\[5.0pt]
0_{\scriptscriptstyle{{m\times n}}}\end{pmatrix}

(205)

Let us now extend
{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
to the same dimensions as
{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
by defining
{\bar{{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}=({\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{\lambdaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\lambdaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})
for some
{\vphantom{\lambdaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\lambdaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m}
. We can then ensure that eq.(
2.1
) through eq.(
204
) hold by using a momenta transformation given by

\displaystyle\tfrac{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\;=\;B^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{--T}}}}}\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\rightarrow\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\begin{pmatrix}{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\
{\vphantom{\lambdaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\lambdaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\end{pmatrix}\;=\;B^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{--T}}}}}\begin{pmatrix}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{u}\end{pmatrix}

(206)

And we have now recovered eq.(
187
) via a different path. To verify that the above does indeed satisfy eq.(
204
), let us partition
B^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}
as follows

\displaystyle B^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}\;=\;\begin{pmatrix}A&N\\[3.0pt]
W&C\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}A\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times n}&,\hskip 9.24994ptN\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times m}\\[5.0pt]
W\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m\times n}&,\hskip 9.24994ptC\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m\times m}\end{array}

For some unknown matrices
A
,
W
,
N
, and
C
. The condition that
BB^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}\!=I
means that these matrices must satisfy

\displaystyle BB^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}\;=\;\begin{pmatrix}\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}A+\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{u}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{u}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}W&\hskip 9.24994pt\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}N+\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{u}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{u}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}C\\[5.0pt]
\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}A+\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{u}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{u}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}W&\hskip 9.24994pt\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}N+\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{u}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{u}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}C\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}{\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{\scriptscriptstyle{{n\times n}}}&\hskip 9.24994pt&0_{\scriptscriptstyle{{n\times m}}}\\[5.0pt]
0_{\scriptscriptstyle{{m\times n}}}&\hskip 9.24994pt&{\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{\scriptscriptstyle{{m\times m}}}\end{pmatrix}

(209)

eq.(
206
) then leads to

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\
{\vphantom{\lambdaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\lambdaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\end{pmatrix}\,=\,B^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{--T}}}}}\begin{pmatrix}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{u}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,=\,A^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,+\,W^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{u}\\[5.0pt]
{\vphantom{\lambdaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\lambdaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,=\,N^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,+\,C^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{u}\end{array}

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\,=\,\begin{pmatrix}A^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}&\,W^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}\end{pmatrix}

And thus eq.(
204
) is satisfied:

\displaystyle B\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}\;=\;\begin{pmatrix}\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}A+\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{u}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{u}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}W\\[5.0pt]
\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}A+\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\varphiup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\varphiup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{u}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{u}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}W\end{pmatrix}\;=\;\begin{pmatrix}{\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{\scriptscriptstyle{{n\times n}}}\\[5.0pt]
0_{\scriptscriptstyle{{m\times n}}}\end{pmatrix}

(212)

where the second equality comes from the first column of eq.(
209
).

2.2  A Family of Canonical Projective Transformations for Particle Dynamics

We now apply the material of section
2.1
to develop a family of canonical point transformations for a generalized version of the projective decomposition.
In this section, we will consider the arbitrary dynamics of a particle in some potential field,
V({\textbf{{{r}}}},t)
, which may be a function of the position/coordinates and, possibly, time but is not a function of the velocity/momenta. We will also use the material in section
A
to include the effects of arbitrary non-conservative forces such as thrust or drag. We will only consider time as the evolution parameter in this section.
Transformations to other evolution parameters will be added when we examine central force motion and the two-body problem in sections
3
and
4
.

The Original Phase Space.

Our starting, minimal, phase space will be the most basic phase space for a single unconstrained particle: cartesian components of the position and velocity vectors in an orthonormal inertial basis.
20
20

20

We say that
{\mkern 1.5mu\hat{\mkern-1.5mu{\textbf{{{i}}}}}}_{i}\in\mathbb{E}^{3}
is an inertial basis if
{\nabla}{\mkern 1.5mu\hat{\mkern-1.5mu{\textbf{{{i}}}}}}=0
and
{\rotatebox[origin]{10.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{t}{\mkern 1.5mu\hat{\mkern-1.5mu{\textbf{{{i}}}}}}=0
. Although not shown here, it is strait forward to show that cartesian position and velocity components in such a basis are indeed canonical coordinates.

We shall denote these as
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
and
{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
, respectively.
That is, Our minimal canonical coordinates, which were called
{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{\piup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\piup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
in section
2.1
, will now be

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{llll}{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;=\;\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}{\text{\footnotesize{ cartesian components of ${\textbf{{{r}}}}$ }}}\\
