{\text{\footnotesize{in some inertial basis}}}\end{array}\right)\hskip 18.49988pt,\hskip 18.49988pt{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;=\;\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}{\text{\footnotesize{ cartesian components of ${\mkern 1.5mu\accentset{\mbox{\scriptsize{.}}}{\mkern-1.5mu{\textbf{{{r}}}}}}$ }}}\\
{\text{\footnotesize{in some inertial basis}}}\end{array}\right)\end{array}

where
r
and
{\mkern 1.5mu\accentset{\mbox{\scriptsize{.}}}{\mkern-1.5mu{\textbf{{{r}}}}}}
are the position (relative to some appropriate origin) and velocity vectors, respectively, for some particle of interest.
The Hamiltonian (per unit mass) and canonical equitation’s of motion — including arbitrary non-conservative forces (per unit mass), denoted
{\textbf{{{a}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}
— for the particle are then given in the
({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})
phase space simply by
21
21

21

Equation eq.(
2.2
) is nothing more newton’s second law,
\ddot{{\textbf{{{r}}}}}=-\nabla_{\hskip-1.70717pt{}}V+{\textbf{{{a}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}
, coordinitized in some inertial basis.

\displaystyle{{K}}({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},t)\;=\;\tfrac{1}{2}{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;+\;V({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},t)

(217)

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}\dot{{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\;=\;\tfrac{\uppartial{{K}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\;=\;{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\[5.0pt]
\dot{{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\;=\;-\tfrac{\uppartial{{K}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\;+\;{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\;=\;-\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\;+\;{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}\end{array}

Where the generalized force (per unit mass),
{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}
, is simply
{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}
— the cartesian components of the non-conservative forces (per unit mass).
Later, in section
3
, we will examine the case that the potential is divided into some dominant central force term,
V^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}(r)
and some arbitrary perturbing term,
V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},t)
. However, we will first consider the arbitrary potential in eq.(
217
).

Canonical Transformations for Different Burdet-Type Projective Decompositions

We seek to develop a canonical formulation of the projective decomposition used by Burdet, Sperling, Vitins, and others, but for the case of arbitrary particle dynamics. We start with the minimal phase space given in eq.(
2.2
) through eq.(
2.2
). We will then specify a point transformation,
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}={\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
, where the new coordinates,
{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{4}
, are non-minimal coordinates
consisting of three coordinates related to the radial unit vector,
{\hat{{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
, and one coordinates related to the radial distance,
r:={\lvert{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\rvert}
. We will then use the developments of section
2.1
to obtain a canonical point transformation from the minimal phase space with canonical coordinates
({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{6}
and Hamiltonian
{{K}}({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},t)
, to a non-minimal phase space with canonical coordinates
({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{8}
and Hamiltonian,
{{H}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)
.
To that end, let us partition our non-minimal canonical coordinates in the manner of eq.(
190
) as

\displaystyle{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\,=\,({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},u)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{4}\qquad,\qquad{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\,=\,({\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},p_{u})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{4}

(220)

where
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
will be related to
{\hat{{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
and where the scalar
u
will be related to
r
.

The Point Transformation,
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
.

In light of eq.(
7
), it may seem like the logical point transformation to use would be
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})=u^{n}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
.
However, for reasons that will become apparent, we will instead use a slight variation of this given as follows
22
22

22

For the remainder of this paper,
n
and
m
are the parameters seen in eq.(
2.2
) and are unrelated to the degrees of freedom or the number of constraints as they were in section
2.1
.

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rllll}{\text{\footnotesize{point transformation}}}:&\qquad{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})\;=\;u^{n}q^{m}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}&\qquad{\textrm{{\footnotesize{rnk}}}\hskip 1.42262pt}\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}=3\\[10.0pt]
{\text{\footnotesize{subject to}}}:&\qquad\varphi({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})\;=\;\tfrac{1}{2}(q^{2}-k^{2})\;=\;0\end{array}

where
q={\lvert{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\rvert}=(q_{1}^{2}+q_{2}^{2}+q_{3}^{2})^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}
denotes the magnitude of
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
, not
{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
, and where we are free to choose the constant parameters
n\neq 0
,
m
, and
k>0
.
With
{{K}}({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},t)
specified in eq.(
217
) and
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
specified in eq.(
2.2
), we now wish to find the momenta transformation,
{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
, and the non-minimal Hamiltonian,
{{H}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)
, such that
{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
and
{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
are canonical coordinates. To accomplish this, we simply apply eq.(
185
) through eq.(
188
) — with
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
taking the place of
{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
, respectively. Before proceeding, let us remark on a few features of the point transformation given by eq.(
2.2
):

\,\scriptscriptstyle{\blacksquare}\,
Remark 2.1

(the value of
k
)
.

We will only consider the case that
k=1
. This leads directly to the general projective decomposition coordinates seen in eq.(
7
):
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}={\hat{{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}

and
u=r^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{n}}}
. However, in the following developments, we will write
k
rather than
1
in order to make it easier for the reader to follow all steps (it is difficult to track the number 1), and also to distinguish between when
q={\lvert{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\rvert}
must be treated as a function of
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and when we are free to treat
q
as the constant
q=k
.

\,\scriptscriptstyle{\blacksquare}\,
Remark 2.2

(the value of
m
)
.

The inclusion of
q^{m}
in eq.(
2.2
) may seem strange given that
\varphi
is specified such that
q=k
is a constant. With
k=1
, the value of
m
has no effect at all on the point transformation itself.
23
23

23

With
k\neq 1
, the term
q^{m}
only contributes to a scaling by an arbitrary constant.

However, the factor of
q^{m}
will affect the the Jacobian
\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}
which plays a role in the transformation of the momenta and Hamiltonian, as seen in eq.(
185
) through eq.(
198
).

\,\scriptscriptstyle{\blacksquare}\,

The parameters
n
,
m
, and
k
will appear throughout section
2.2
and may make some equations look temporarily cluttered. We will choose desirable values for these parameters in sections
3
and
4
which will simplify things considerably.

The Momenta Transformation,
{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
.

To find the momenta transformation we need the matrix
B
defined in eq.(
186
) or eq.(
198
). The following partials of
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
and
\varphi({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})
from eq.(
2.2
) are needed:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,u^{n}q^{m-2}(q^{2}{\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}+m{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\otimes{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})\,=\,u^{n}q^{m}({\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}+m{\hat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\otimes{\hat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})&\;\;,\\[6.0pt]
\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial u}\,=\,nu^{n-1}q^{m}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}&\;\;,\end{array}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}&\tfrac{\uppartial\varphi}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\[6.0pt]
&\tfrac{\uppartial\varphi}{\uppartial u}\,=\,0\end{array}

Where
{\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}
is the
3\times 3
identity matrix, where
{\hat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}=\tfrac{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{q}
, and where, in this context, the tensor product symbol,
\otimes
, denotes an outer product such that
({\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\otimes{\vphantom{b}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{b}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}){\vphantom{c}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{c}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}={\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\vphantom{b}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{b}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{c}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{c}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})
.
The
B^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}
matrix appearing in equation eq.(
187
), and its inverse, are then given by

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}B^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})\;:=\begin{pmatrix}u^{n}q^{m}({\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}+m{\hat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\otimes{\hat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})&\;\;\;\;{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\[5.0pt]
nu^{n-1}q^{m}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}&\;\;\;\;0\end{pmatrix}\hskip 18.49988pt,\hskip 18.49988ptB^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{--T}}}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})\;=\begin{pmatrix}\tfrac{1}{u^{n}q^{m}}({\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}-{\hat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\otimes{\hat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})&\;\;\;\;\tfrac{u^{1-n}}{nq^{m+2}}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\[5.0pt]
\tfrac{1}{q^{2}}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}&\;\;\;\;-\tfrac{m+1}{n}\tfrac{u}{q^{2}}\end{pmatrix}\end{array}

eq.(
187
), or eq.(
198
), then leads to the momenta transformation for
{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
as well as
\lambda({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
:

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\
\lambda\end{pmatrix}=B^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{--T}}}}}\begin{pmatrix}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\
p_{u}\end{pmatrix}\;\;:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})\;=\;\tfrac{1}{u^{n}q^{m}}\big[{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}-({\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}){\hat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\;+\;\tfrac{u}{nq}p_{u}{\hat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\big]\\[8.0pt]
\lambda({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})\;=\;\tfrac{1}{q^{2}}({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}-\tfrac{m+1}{n}up_{u})\end{array}

It will also be useful to express the specific angular momentum,
\bm{\ell}={\textbf{{{r}}}}\times{\mkern 1.5mu\accentset{\mbox{\scriptsize{.}}}{\mkern-1.5mu{\textbf{{{r}}}}}}
, in terms of
{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
and
{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
. It is straightforward to show that the components of this vector in the inertial basis, denoted
{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
, and the magnitude,
{\ell}
, are given in terms of
({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})
by

\displaystyle{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,=\,{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\times{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}

,

\displaystyle{\ell}^{2}\,=\,({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\times{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\times{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})\,=\,r^{2}{\nu}^{2}-({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})^{2}

(230)

Interestingly, substitution of
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
and
{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
from eq.(
2.2
) and eq.(
2.2
) into the above leads to expressions of the precisely the same form:

\displaystyle{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})\,=\,{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\times{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}

,

\displaystyle{\ell}^{2}({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})\,=\,({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\times{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\times{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})\,=\,q^{2}p^{2}-({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})^{2}

(231)

The above does not depend on
u
or
p_{u}
and holds for any
n\neq 0
and
m
in eq.(
2.2
). Note that we have
not
simplified any of the above expressions using

.

The Hamiltonian,
{{H}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)
.

The Hamiltonian for
{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
and
{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
is found using eq.(
188
). The point transformation and constraint given by eq.(
2.2
) are not explicit functions of time. Therefore, it is seen from eq.(
188
) that the new Hamiltonian is found from direct substitution of
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
and
{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
into the original Hamiltonian for the minimal coordinates,
{{K}}
. That is,

\displaystyle{{H}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)\;=\;{{K}}\big({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}),{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}),t\big)

(232)

where
{{K}}
is given by eq.(
217
),
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
by eq.(
2.2
), and
{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
by eq.(
2.2
). From these last two relations, we see that

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rl}r^{2}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})&\;=\;{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;=\;u^{2n}q^{2m}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;=\;u^{2n}q^{2m+2}\\[8.0pt]
{\nu}^{2}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})&\;=\;{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;=\;\tfrac{1}{u^{2n}q^{2m+2}}\Big(q^{2}p^{2}-({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})^{2}\;+\;\tfrac{1}{n^{2}}u^{2}p_{u}^{2}\Big)\end{array}

Substitution into
{{K}}
, given in eq.(
217
), leads to the new Hamiltonian:

\displaystyle{{H}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)\;=\;\tfrac{1}{u^{2n}q^{2m+2}}\frac{1}{2}\Big(q^{2}p^{2}-({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})^{2}+\tfrac{1}{n^{2}}u^{2}p_{u}^{2}\Big)\,+\,V({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)

(235)

The Inverse Transformations & Meaning of
{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})
and
{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})
.

We have yet to show the physical meaning of the new canonical coordinates,
{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
and
{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
, in terms of
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
.
Note that
\varphi=0
in eq.(
2.2
) is equivalent to
k=q
. Thus, eq.(
2.2
) and eq.(
2.2
) together provide eight equations for
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
,
k({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})
,
{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
and
\lambda({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
in terms of the eight coordinates
{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}=({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},u)
and
{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}=({\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},p_{u})
.
These may be inverted to obtain the inverse relations for
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
,
u
,
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
, and
p_{u}
in terms of
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
,
k
,
{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
, and
\lambda
.
The point transformation given by eq.(
2.2
) — which is independent of the momenta and
\lambda
— is easily inverted for
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})
and
u({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})

by first finding
r({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
and
{\hat{{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
and then inverting these expressions, along with the relation
k=q
, to express
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
u
in terms of
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
k
:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}r({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})\,=\,|{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}|\,=\,u^{n}q^{m+1}\\[6.0pt]
{\hat{{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})\,=\,\tfrac{{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{r}\,=\,\tfrac{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{q}\end{array}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\;\;k\;=\;q\;\;}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}u({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})\,=\,\frac{r^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{n}}}}{q^{\nicefrac{{(m+1)}}{{n}}}}\,=\,\frac{r^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{n}}}}{k^{\nicefrac{{(m+1)}}{{n}}}}\\[6.0pt]
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})\,=\,q\tfrac{{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{r}\,=\,k{\hat{{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\end{array}

we can verify from the above that
k=1
leads directly to the the desired projective decomposition coordinates,
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}={\hat{{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
and
u=r^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{n}}}
, as was claimed in Remark
2.1
.
Now, the relation for the momenta,
{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
, is found from eq.(
187
), or eq.(
198
), as:

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\
p_{u}\end{pmatrix}\;=\;B^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}\begin{pmatrix}{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\
\lambda\end{pmatrix}\quad:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;=\;u^{n}q^{m}\big[{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}+m({\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}){\hat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\big]\;+\;\lambda{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\[8.0pt]
p_{u}\;=\;nu^{n-1}q^{m}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\end{array}

which is simply the inverse of eq.(
2.2
).
We then substitute
{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})
from eq.(
2.2
) to express the above in terms of
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
,
{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
,
k
and
\lambda
:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rll}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})&\;=\;\tfrac{r}{k}\big[{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}+m({\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}){\hat{{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\big]\;+\;\lambda k{\hat{{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}&\;\;=\;\;\tfrac{r}{k}({\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}+m\dot{r}{\hat{{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})\;+\;\lambda k{\hat{{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\\[8.0pt]
p_{u}({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})&\;=\;n\big(\tfrac{k^{m+1}}{r}\big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{n}}}{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}&\;\;=\;\;nk^{\nicefrac{{(m+1)}}{{n}}}\,r^{\nicefrac{{(n-1)}}{{n}}}\,\dot{r}\end{array}

Equations eq.(
2.2
) and eq.(
2.2
) for
{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})
and
{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})
tell us the physical meaning of the new coordinates and momenta. These are the only equations thus far where we have used the relation
q=k
.

Some Comments.

Before proceeding, let us remark on a few features of the canonical point transformation outlined in eq.(
2.2
) through eq.(
2.2
).

\,\scriptscriptstyle{\blacksquare}\,
Remark 2.3
.

The Hamiltonian given by eq.(
235
) should be constructed and differentiated treating all
{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
and
{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
as eight independent coordinates. That is, the Hamiltonian should
not
be simplified using the constraint
q=k
. Instead, we must treat
q={\lvert{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\rvert}
as a function of
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
such that
\tfrac{\uppartial q}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}={\hat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
.
Failing to do this will lead to incorrect partial derivatives of
{{H}}
and thus incorrect equations of motion.
It is for this reason that equations for
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
and
{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
— eq.(
2.2
) and eq.(
2.2
) — were left as functions of
q={\lvert{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\rvert}
.

\,\scriptscriptstyle{\blacksquare}\,
Remark 2.4
.

It will later be proven in section
2.4
that
q={\lvert{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\rvert}
and
\lambda({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
, which is given by eq.(
2.2
),
are integrals of motion.
That is,
q
and
\lambda({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
are conserved constants along all trajectories of the canonical equations of motion (which we will soon find). It will be shown that this holds in the presence of both conservative and non-conservative forces.
We are only interested in the case that these constants are given by
q=1
and
\lambda=0
such that our eight canonical coordinates, which are over-parameterized by two, then satisfy the following two additional integrals of motion:

\displaystyle q\;=\;k\;=\;1\hskip 18.49988pt,

\displaystyle{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}-\tfrac{m+1}{n}up_{u}\;=\;\lambda\;=\;0

(244)

Although not necessary, we may use the above integrals of motion to simplify the equations of motion
after
they have been found from the Hamiltonian.

\,\scriptscriptstyle{\blacksquare}\,
Remark 2.5
.

We do not need to do anything to enforce the above relations that
q=1
and
\lambda=0
other than specify our initial conditions such that these relations hold at the initial time. That is, for some known initial conditions
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
and
{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
, we specify
{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
and
{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
using eq.(
2.2
) and eq.(
2.2
) with
k=1
and
\lambda=0
:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{cc}{\text{\footnotesize{choosing}}}\\
q=k=1,\\
{\text{\footnotesize{and}}}\;\;\lambda=0\end{array}\hskip 9.24994pt\left\{\hskip 9.24994pt\begin{array}[]{ll}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})\;=\;{\hat{{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}&,\\[8.0pt]
u({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})\;=\;r^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{n}}}&,\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})\;=\;r({\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}+m\dot{r}{\hat{{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})\\[8.0pt]
p_{u}({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})\;=\;nr^{\nicefrac{{(n-1)}}{{n}}}\,\dot{r}\end{array}

Then, since
q
and
\lambda
are integrals of motion, the above relations hold for all time and tell us the physical meaning of the new canonical coordinates. Note that the meaning of
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
changes depending on
m
but the meaning of
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
does not. Thus, our non-minimal canonical coordinates have the peculiar feature the same coordinates may have different conjugate momenta.

\,\scriptscriptstyle{\blacksquare}\,
Remark 2.6
.

For the parameter
m
in eq.(
2.2
), there are really only two values of interest:
m=0
and
m=-1
. The former corresponds to the classic projective decomposition seen in eq.(
7
),
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})=u^{n}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
. The latter corresponds to
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})=u^{n}{\hat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
, which may seem redundant given that we set
q=1
and thus
m
does not affect the physical meaning of
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}={\hat{{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
or
u=r^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{n}}}
. However, as seen in eq.(
2.2
) through eq.(
2.5
),
m

does
effect the physical meaning of
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})
and
\lambda({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
, as well as the functional dependence of
r({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
and
{{H}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)
on
q={\lvert{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\rvert}
.
For example, note a few of the differences that follow from
m=0
versus
m=-1
:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{cc}{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})=u^{n}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\[3.0pt]
\text{\footnotesize{$(m=0)$}}\end{array}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}r({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})=u^{n}{\lvert{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\rvert}\\[5.0pt]
\lambda={\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}-\tfrac{up_{u}}{n}=0\\[5.0pt]
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=r{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\[5.0pt]
p^{2}={\ell}^{2}+r^{2}\dot{r}^{2}\end{array}\right.\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\left|\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\begin{array}[]{cc}{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})=u^{n}{\hat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\\[3.0pt]
\text{\footnotesize{$(m=-1)$}}\end{array}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}r({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})=u^{n}\\[5.0pt]
\lambda={\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=0\\[5.0pt]
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=r^{2}\dot{{\hat{{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}={\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\times{\hat{{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\\[5.0pt]
p^{2}={\ell}^{2}\end{array}\right.\right.

The properties on the right-hand-side of the above — that
r
is only a function of
u
, that
p={\ell}
, and that
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
,
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
, and
{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
are all orthogonal — will lead to several desirable features compared to the
m=0
case.
For the remainder of this section, we will continue to consider arbitrary values of
m
to show that the developments hold for any
m
. We will choose
m=-1
starting in section
3
.

2.3  Particle Dynamics with Arbitrary Forces

General Canonical EOMs with Non-Conservative Forces.

Consider the
2N
-dim phase space for a “particle” in
\mathbb{E}^{\scriptscriptstyle{N}}
with some Hamiltonian,
H(x^{i},y_{i},t)
in
arbitrary canonical coordinates
(x^{i},y_{i})
, for
i=1,\dots,N
. Suppose some non-conservative forces,
{\textbf{{{a}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}
, act on the system. Let
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{N}}
be inertial cartesian coordinate vectors, with
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
implicitly treated as functions on phase space,
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}(x^{i},y_{i})
.
The governing equation for any function (possibly time-dependent) on phase space,
f(x^{i},y_{i},t)
, may be obtained from the Hamiltonian and the map
({\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{y}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{y}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})\mapsto{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
using the Poisson bracket as

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rlll}\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}f(x^{i},y_{i},t)&=\;\{f,H\}\,-\,\{f,r^{j}\}a_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}\,+\,\tfrac{\uppartial f}{\uppartial t}\;=\;\{f,H\}\,+\,\tfrac{\uppartial f}{\uppartial{\vphantom{y}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{y}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{y}\,-\,\tfrac{\uppartial f}{\uppartial{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{x}\,+\,\tfrac{\uppartial f}{\uppartial t}\end{array}

where
{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{x}={\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{y}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{y}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}
and
{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{y}={\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}
.
It must be stressed that the above
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=(r^{1},\dots,r^{\scriptscriptstyle{N}})
is the
inertial cartesian coordinate vector
(relative to some fixed origin) such that each
r^{j}
is, in general, a function of all
2N
canonical coordinates. However, the following deals only with canonical transformations induced by point transformations (that is, coordinate transformations on configuration space lifted to the cotangent bundle) such that
\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial y_{i}}=0
and
{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{x}=0
always holds.

Canonical EOMs for Projective Coordinates.

We now return our attention to Hamiltonian dynamics in 3-dim space formulated in terms of our projective coordinates.
Suppose that, in addition to arbitrary conservative forces, one or several non-conservative forces such as thrust or drag also act on the particle.
Then, as shown in section
A
, an additional term is included in the momenta equation such that the canonical equations of motion are given by

\displaystyle\dot{{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\;=\;\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\qquad\qquad,\qquad\qquad\dot{{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\;=\;-\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\;+\;{\bar{{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}

(265)

where
{\bar{{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}=({\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},\alpha_{u})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{4}
is the generalized force (per unit reduced mass) arising from any non-conservative forces and will be discussed further in section
2.3
.
With the Hamiltonian given by eq.(
235
), the above leads to the following equations of motion

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}&\dot{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{u^{2n}q^{2m+2}}\big[q^{2}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}-({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}){\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\big]\\[10.0pt]
&\dot{u}\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial p_{u}}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{u^{2n}q^{2m+2}}\tfrac{1}{n^{2}}u^{2}p_{u}\end{array}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}&\dot{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,-\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}+{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{u^{2n}q^{2m+4}}\Big(\big[mq^{2}p^{2}-(m+1)({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})^{2}+\tfrac{m+1}{n^{2}}u^{2}p_{u}^{2}\big]{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}+q^{2}({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}){\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\Big)\,-\,\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,+\,{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\[10.0pt]
&\dot{p}_{u}\,=\,-\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial u}+\alpha_{u}\,=\,\tfrac{n}{u^{2n+1}q^{2m+2}}\big[q^{2}p^{2}-({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})^{2}\,+\,\tfrac{n-1}{n^{3}}u^{2}p_{u}^{2}\big]\,-\,\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial u}\,+\,\alpha_{u}\end{array}

However, as per Remark
2.4
, the above may be simplified using
q=k
for any constant
k>0
. Specifically, for
q=1
this leads to

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}&\dot{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{u^{2n}}\big[{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}-({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}){\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\big]\\[10.0pt]
&\dot{u}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{n^{2}}\tfrac{1}{u^{2n-2}}p_{u}\end{array}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}&\dot{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{u^{2n}}\Big(\big[mp^{2}-(m+1)({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})^{2}+\tfrac{m+1}{n^{2}}u^{2}p_{u}^{2}\big]{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}+({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}){\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\Big)\,-\,\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,+\,{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\[10.0pt]
&\dot{p}_{u}\,=\,\tfrac{n}{u^{2n+1}}\big[p^{2}-({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})^{2}\,+\,\tfrac{n-1}{n^{3}}u^{2}p_{u}^{2}\big]\,-\,\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial u}\,+\,\alpha_{u}\end{array}

The equations of motion for the coordinates,
{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
, involve only kinematics. The forces only appear in the equations of motion for the momenta,
{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
. All conservative forces (per unit mass) acting on the system will appear only in
\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}
. All non-conservative forces will appear only in
{\bar{{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
.
24
24

24

Since we are working with Hamiltonians and momenta
per unit mass
, the terms
\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}
and
{\bar{{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
will contain acceleration terms (forces per unit mass) rather than force terms.

\,\scriptscriptstyle{\blacksquare}\,

Although the above dynamics look rather hideous, with no obvious improvement over the original cartesian coordinate formulation, they will soon be simplified tremendously with an appropriate choice of
m
and
n
, along with a transformation of the evolution parameter.

The Conservative Force Terms,
\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}

For some specified potential,
V
, there are two methods we may use when calculating the conservative force terms,
\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}
, appearing in the equations of motion, eq.(
2.3
).

Method 1.

The first method is to simply substitute
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
from eq.(
2.2
) into
V({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},t)
in order to obtain an expression for
V({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)
. One may then directly calculate
\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}
. If using this method, note from Remark
2.3
that
V({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)
should
not
be simplified using
q=1
.
For example, consider an unperturbed central force potential of the form
V({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})={\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}r^{b}
for some constants

–

\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}

and
b
.
Substituting into this
r({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
from eq.(
2.2
) leads to
V({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
given by

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rl}{\text{\footnotesize{example}}}:&\hskip 9.24994ptV(r)\,=\,{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.09999pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}r^{b}\;\;=\;\;V({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})\;=\;{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.09999pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}(u^{n}q^{m+1})^{b}\end{array}

We may then directly calculate
\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}
and
\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial u}
from the above:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lll}\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\;=\;\tfrac{b(m+1)}{q^{2}}{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.09999pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}(u^{n}q^{m+1})^{b}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}&\;=\;b(m+1){\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.09999pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}u^{nb}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\[6.0pt]
\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial u}\;=\;\tfrac{bn}{u}{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.09999pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}(u^{n}q^{m+1})^{b}&\;=\;nb{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.09999pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}u^{nb-1}\end{array}

Where, as per Remark
2.4
, the form on the far right-hand-side has been simplified using
q=1

after
taking the derivatives.
If we had simplified the potential itself in eq.(
2.3
) using
q=1
as
V({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})={\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}u^{nb}
, then we would have incorrectly obtained
\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}={\vphantom{0}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{0}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
.

Method 2.

Alternatively, if we already know the gradient of
V({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},t)
with respect to the minimal coordinates,
\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}
, then we may express

\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}
and
\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial u}
using

\displaystyle\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\;\,=\,\;\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\quad,

\displaystyle\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial u}\;\,=\,\;\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial u}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}

(286)

Substituting the the partials from eq.(
2.2
), the above leads to
25
25

25

Note that
-\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=-\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial r}
is simply the radial component of the acceleration from conservative forces.

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lll}\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\;\,=\,\;u^{n}q^{m}\big[\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\;+\;\tfrac{m}{q^{2}}(\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}){\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\big]&\;\;\;=\;u^{n}\big[\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\;+\;m(\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}){\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\big]\\[10.0pt]
\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial u}\;\,=\,\;nu^{n-1}q^{m}\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}&\;\;\;=\;nu^{n-1}\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\end{array}

Where, as per Remark
2.4
, the form on the far right-hand-side has been simplified using
q=1
.
Note that eq.(
2.3
)
follows directly from the point transformation eq.(
2.2
) and holds regardless of the meaning of the minimal coordinates,
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
. However, in the particular case that
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
is the three cartesian components of the position vector in an inertial basis, then
-\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}
is simply the components of the conservative force vector (per unit mass),
-\nabla_{\hskip-1.70717pt{}}V
, in that same basis:

\displaystyle-\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\;=\;\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}{\text{\footnotesize{ cartesian components of the conservative forces }}}\\
{\text{\footnotesize{(per unit mass), $\,-\nabla_{\hskip-1.70717pt{}}V$, in the inertial basis}}}\end{array}\right)

For example, for the unperturbed central force example give in eq.(
2.3
),
V({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})={\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}r^{b}
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rl}{\text{\footnotesize{example }}}:&\hskip 18.49988pt-\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,-b{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.09999pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}r^{b-1}{\hat{{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\,=\,-\tfrac{1}{q}b{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.09999pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}(u^{n}q^{m+1})^{b-1}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,=\,-b{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.09999pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}u^{n(b-1)}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\end{array}

where the form on the far right-hand-side has been simplified using
q=1
.
It can be verified that substituting the above into eq.(
2.3
) leads to the same result as in eq.(
2.3
).

The Non-Conservative Force Terms,
{\bar{{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}

Let the vector
{\textbf{{{a}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}
be the sum of the accelerations resulting from non-conservative forces and let
{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}
be the cartesian components of this vector in the inertial basis.
Then, as seen in appendix
A
, the generalized accelerations,
{\bar{{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{4}
, appearing in the equation for
\dot{{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}
are given in terms of
{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}
by

\displaystyle{\bar{{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}

(297)

Partitioning
{\bar{{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
in the usual manner as
{\bar{{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}=({\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},\alpha_{u})
and using the partials given in eq.(
2.2
), the above leads to

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{llll}{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}&\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}&\,=\,u^{n}q^{m}\big[{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}\,+\,\tfrac{m}{q^{2}}({\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}){\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\big]&\,=\,u^{n}\big[{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}\,+\,m({\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}){\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\big]\\[10.0pt]
\alpha_{u}&\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial u}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}&\,=\,nu^{n-1}q^{m}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}&\,=\,nu^{n-1}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\end{array}

where, as before, the for right-hand-side has been simplified using
q=1
.
The above expressions for
{\bar{{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
in terms of the
non-conservative
acceleration,
{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}
, are the same as
the expressions for
-\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}
in terms of the
conservative
acceleration,
-\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}
, given in eq.(
2.3
). Therefore, if we define the sum of conservative and non-conservative accelerations as

\displaystyle{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;:=\;-\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\;+\;{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}\;=\;\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}{\text{\footnotesize{cartesian components of the {total}}}}\\
{\text{\footnotesize{force (per unit mass) from conservative}}}\\
{\text{\footnotesize{and non-conservative sources}}}\end{array}\right)

then, from eq.(
2.3
) and eq.(
2.3
), the terms arising from all forces may be combined as
26
26

26

Note that
{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=a_{r}
is the radial component of the acceleration from all forces.

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lll}-\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,+\,{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}&\;\;\,=\,u^{n}q^{m}\big[{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,+\,\tfrac{m}{q^{2}}({\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}){\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\big]&\;\;\;=\;u^{n}\big[{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}+m({\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}){\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\big]\\[8.0pt]
-\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial u}\,+\,\alpha_{u}&\;\;\,=\,nu^{n-1}q^{m}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}&\;\;\;=\;nu^{n-1}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\end{array}

Equations of Motion Using Poisson Brackets

Hamiltonian formalism provides a convenient way to find the equation of motion for
any
function of the canonical coordinates.
Consider any two scalar-valued functions, say
f({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)
and
g({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)
, which may be considered as functions of the canonical coordinates and, possibly, time.
The Poisson bracket of
f
and
g
, which we shall denote as
\{f,g\}
, is defined as

\displaystyle\{f,g\}\;:=\;\tfrac{\uppartial f}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\tfrac{\uppartial g}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\;-\;\tfrac{\uppartial f}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\tfrac{\uppartial g}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}

(305)

Now, the time derivative of some function
f({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)
is given by

\displaystyle\dot{f}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)\;=\;\tfrac{\uppartial f}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\dot{{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\;+\;\tfrac{\uppartial f}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\dot{{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\;+\;\tfrac{\uppartial f}{\uppartial t}\;=\;\tfrac{\uppartial f}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\;-\;\tfrac{\uppartial f}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\;+\;\tfrac{\uppartial f}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\bar{{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\;+\;\tfrac{\uppartial f}{\uppartial t}

(306)

where we have used eq.(
265
).
The first two terms on the far right-hand-side of the above is precisely the Poisson bracket of
f
with
{{H}}
. Thus, the equation of motion for
any
function of the canonical coordinates is given by

\displaystyle\dot{f}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)\;=\;\{f,{{H}}\}\;+\;\tfrac{\uppartial f}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\bar{{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\;+\;\tfrac{\uppartial f}{\uppartial t}

(307)

where the second term need only be included if non-conservative forces act on the system and the last term need only be included if
f
is an explicit function of time.
Note that if
f=q_{i}
or
f=p_{i}
, then the above simply reproduces the canonical equations of motion. The above expression will be used in section
2.4
to prove that
q
and
\lambda
are integrals of motion for the Hamiltonian given in eq.(
235
).

2.4  Derivation of the Two Additional “Integrals of Motion”

It was claimed in Remark
2.4
that
q={\lvert{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\rvert}
and
\lambda=\tfrac{1}{q^{2}}({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}-\tfrac{m+1}{n}up_{u})
are integrals of motion (i.e., constant along all possible trajectories). We will now prove this by showing that
\dot{q}=0
and
\dot{\lambda}=0
using the Poisson bracket expression of eq.(
307
).

Proof that
q={\lvert{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\rvert}
is constant.

Noting that
\tfrac{\uppartial q}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}={\bar{{\vphantom{0}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{0}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
and
\tfrac{\uppartial q}{\uppartial t}=0
, it is strait forward to see that eq.(
307
) leads to
\dot{q}=0
:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rl}\dot{q}\,=\,\{q,{{H}}\}&\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial q}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\,-\,\cancel{\tfrac{\uppartial q}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{q}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\\[8.0pt]
&\,=\,\tfrac{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{q}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\big(\tfrac{1}{u^{2n}q^{2m+2}}\big[q^{2}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}-({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}){\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\big]\big)\,=\,0\end{array}

where we have used
{{H}}
given by eq.(
235
).
Therefore, the “constraint”
q=k
(for any constant
k>0
) is, in fact, an integral of motion that is automatically conserved by the equations of motion.

Proof that
\lambda
is constant.

Consider
\dot{\lambda}
as given by the Poisson bracket expression of eq.(
307
):

\displaystyle\dot{\lambda}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})\;=\;\{\lambda,{{H}}\}\;+\;\tfrac{\uppartial\lambda}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\bar{{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\;=\;\tfrac{\uppartial\lambda}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\;-\;\tfrac{\uppartial\lambda}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\;+\;\tfrac{\uppartial\lambda}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\bar{{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}

(310)

Where
\lambda({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
is given by eq.(
2.2
) as
\lambda=\tfrac{1}{q^{2}}({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}-\tfrac{m+1}{n}up_{u})
. The above then leads to
\dot{\lambda}
given by

\displaystyle\dot{\lambda}

\displaystyle\,=\,\{\tfrac{1}{q^{2}}({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}-\tfrac{m+1}{n}up_{u}),{{H}}\}\;+\;{\bar{{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\tfrac{\uppartial}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}-\tfrac{m+1}{n}up_{u})\tfrac{1}{q^{2}}

(311)
