\displaystyle\,=\,\tfrac{1}{q^{2}}\Big(({\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}-2\lambda{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,-\,\tfrac{m+1}{n}p_{u}\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial p_{u}}\,-\,{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,+\,\tfrac{m+1}{n}u\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial u}\,+\,{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,-\,\tfrac{m+1}{n}u\alpha_{u}\Big)

\displaystyle\,=\,\tfrac{1}{q^{2}}\Bigg\{\tfrac{1}{u^{2n}q^{2m+2}}\Big(\big[q^{2}p^{2}-({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})^{2}-\tfrac{m+1}{n}\tfrac{1}{n^{2}}u^{2}p_{u}^{2}\big]-2\lambda\big[q^{2}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}-{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}q^{2}\big]\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\;\;\dots\,+\,\tfrac{1}{u^{2n}q^{2m+2}}\big[mq^{2}p^{2}-m({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})^{2}+\tfrac{m+1}{n^{2}}u^{2}p_{u}^{2}\big]-\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\;\;\dots\,-\,\tfrac{m+1}{u^{2n}q^{2m+2}}\big[q^{2}p^{2}-({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})^{2}+\tfrac{n-1}{n^{3}}u^{2}p_{u}^{2}\big]+\tfrac{m+1}{n}u\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial u}\;+\;{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}-\tfrac{m+1}{n}u\alpha_{u}\Bigg\}

Simplifying and collecting like terms, we obtain

\displaystyle\dot{\lambda}

\displaystyle\;=\;\tfrac{1}{u^{2n}q^{2m+4}}\Big\{\cancel{[1+m-(m+1)]}\big[q^{2}p^{2}-({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})^{2}\big]\;+\;\tfrac{(m+1)}{n^{2}}u^{2}p_{u}^{2}\cancel{(1-\tfrac{1}{n}-\tfrac{n-1}{n})}\Big\}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\;\;\dots\;+\;\tfrac{1}{q^{2}}(\tfrac{m+1}{n}u\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial u}\;-\;\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})\;-\;\tfrac{1}{q^{2}}(\tfrac{m+1}{n}u\alpha_{u}\;-\;{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})

It is seen that the entire first line in brackets — the terms arising purely from the kinematics — reduces to zero, leaving only the terms involving
\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}
and
{\bar{{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
— the terms arising from conservative and non-conservative forces, respectively. However, from eq.(
2.3
) and eq.(
2.3
), we see that these terms also cancel:

\displaystyle\tfrac{m+1}{n}u\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial u}-\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}

\displaystyle\;\;=\;\;\tfrac{m+1}{n}uu^{n-1}q^{m}\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;-\;u^{n}q^{m}(m+1)\tfrac{\uppartial V}{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;\;=\;\;0

(312)

\displaystyle\tfrac{m+1}{n}u\alpha_{u}\;-\;{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}

\displaystyle\;\;=\;\;\tfrac{m+1}{n}uu^{n-1}q^{m}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;-\;u^{n}q^{m}(m+1){\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;\;=\;\;0

(313)

The entire right-hand-side of eq.(
311
) then reduces to zero and, therefore,
\lambda
is an integral of motion:

\displaystyle\dot{\lambda}\;=\;\{\lambda,{{H}}\}\;+\;\tfrac{\uppartial\lambda}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\bar{{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\;=\;0

(314)

Note that this holds regardless of whether the forces involved are conservative or non-conservative and that the derivation did not require us to assume
q=k
at any point.

\,\scriptscriptstyle{\blacksquare}\,
Remark 2.7
.

We may choose our initial conditions such that
q=1
(or
q=k
for any
k>0
) and
\lambda=0
at the initial time. The equations of motion will then automatically conserve the following integrals of motion:
27
27

27

It can also be noted that taking a Lagrangian approach and applying the method of Broucke also leads to
\lambda=0

[
33
]
.

\displaystyle q\;=\;k\;=\;1\hskip 18.49988pt,

\displaystyle{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;-\;\tfrac{m+1}{n}up_{u}\;=\;\lambda\;=\;0

(315)

Although not
necessary
, we may use the above to simplify the equations of motion. We have already been doing this with
q=1
but it was not proven until now that this is “allowed”.

3.  REGULARIZED CENTRAL FORCE DYNAMICS WITH PROJECTIVE COORDINATES

We
†
†

Some of the content of this chapter has appeared previously in conference papers by myself (Peterson) , Majji, and Junkins
[
28
,
31
]
. It is published here with their permission.

will now assume that the potential,
V({\textbf{{{r}}}},t)
is divided into a dominant central force component,
V^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}(r)
, and a weaker component,
V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}({\textbf{{{r}}}},t)
, which may include arbitrary conservative perturbations (e.g., the
J_{2}
potential and/or third body potential) We may then write
V({\textbf{{{r}}}},t)
as

\displaystyle V({\textbf{{{r}}}},t)\;=\;V^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}(r)\;+\;V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}({\textbf{{{r}}}},t)

(316)

such that the Hamiltonian for the minimal
({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})
phase space, eq.(
217
), becomes
28
28

28

As before,
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
are the cartesian components of the position and velocity vectors in an inertial basis.

\displaystyle{{K}}({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},t)\;=\;\tfrac{1}{2}{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;+\;V^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}(r)\;+\;V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},t)

(317)

We now apply the canonical projective decomposition outlined in section
2.2
. As per Remark
2.6
, we will, for the remainder of this work, choose
m=-1
in the point transformation of eq.(
2.2
). Thus, we will begin our analysis of central force motion by applying the key developments of section
2.2
for the case that
m=-1
and highlighting some of the useful features and simplifications that follow from this choice along the way.
Then, in section
3.3
, we will use the extended phase space (section
B
) to introduce a transformation of the evolution parameter to something other than time. This will then be used in sections
3.4
and
3.4
to obtain equations of motion that are fully linear for pure central force motion.

3.1  The Canonical Point Transformation (with
m=–1
)

Using the developments of section
2.2
, we specify a point transformation from cartesian position coordinates in the inertial basis,
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
, to non-minimal coordinates
{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}=({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},u)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{4}
given by eq.(
2.2
) with
m=-1\,
(and
k=1
) as

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rl}{\text{\footnotesize{point transformation}}}:&\qquad{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})\;=\;\tfrac{u^{n}}{q}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,=\,u^{n}{\hat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\\[10.0pt]
{\text{\footnotesize{subject to}}}:&\qquad\varphi({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})\;=\;\tfrac{1}{2}(q^{2}-1)\;=\;0\end{array}

where
{\hat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}=\tfrac{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{q}
. The appropriate momenta transformation for
{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
, along with the multiplier
\lambda({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
,
is then given by eq.(
2.2
) with
m=-1
as

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rlr}{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})&=\;\tfrac{1}{qu^{n}}\big[q^{2}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}-({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}){\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}+\tfrac{1}{n}up_{u}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\big]&,\qquad\qquad\lambda({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})\;=\;\tfrac{1}{q^{2}}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\end{array}

It was shown in eq.(
231
) that the
the specific angular momentum,
{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}={\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\times{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
, does not depend on
u
,
p_{u}
,
n
, or
m
, and is given solely in terms of
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
by

\displaystyle{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})\;=\;{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\times{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\qquad,

\displaystyle{\ell}^{2}({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})\;=\;q^{2}p^{2}\;-\;({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})^{2}

(321)

As previously shown, the new Hamiltonian
{{H}}
is found by substitution of
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
and
{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
— now given by eq.(
3.1
) and eq.(
3.1
) — into the classic Hamiltonian for
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
which is now given by eq.(
317
). This leads to

\displaystyle{{H}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)\,=\,\tfrac{1}{u^{2n}}\tfrac{1}{2}\Big(q^{2}p^{2}-({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})^{2}\,+\,\tfrac{1}{n^{2}}u^{2}p_{u}^{2}\Big)\,+\,V^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}(u)\,+\,V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)

(322)

It was shown in section
2.4
that
q=|{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}|
and
\lambda({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
— now given by eq.(
3.1
) — are integrals of motion for the above Hamiltonian and that we may specify our initial conditions such that these constants are given by
1
and
0
, respectively. Our eight canonical coordinates then automatically satisfy two integrals of motion given by

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}{\text{\footnotesize{integrals of motion:}}}\end{array}

\displaystyle q\,=\,|{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}|\,=\,1\quad,

\displaystyle{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,=\,\lambda\,=\,0

Thus,
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
are orthogonal along all trajectories (this does not hold for
m\neq-1
).
The physical meaning of the new canonical coordinates,
{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}=({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},u)
and
{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}=({\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},p_{u})
, is given in terms of
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
by eq.(
2.2
) and eq.(
2.2
). With
q=1
,
m=-1
, and
\lambda=0
, these become
29
29

29

If we were to instead use
m=0
such that
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})=u^{n}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
, then the conjugate momenta would be
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=r{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
.

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})\;=\;{\hat{{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}&,\\[8.0pt]
u({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})\;=\;r^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{n}}}&,\end{array}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})\;=\;r({\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}-\dot{r}{\hat{{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})\;=\;r^{2}\dot{{\hat{{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\;=\;{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\times{\hat{{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\\[8.0pt]
p_{u}({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})\;=\;nr^{\nicefrac{{(n-1)}}{{n}}}\,\dot{r}\end{array}

Note that two common choices of
n
for the projective decomposition,
n=1
and
n=-1
, lead to the following
u
and
p_{u}
:

\displaystyle n=1\;\left\{\quad\begin{array}[]{ll}u\;=\;r\\[4.0pt]
p_{u}\;=\;\dot{r}\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle n=-1\;\left\{\quad\begin{array}[]{ll}u\;=\;\tfrac{1}{r}\\[4.0pt]
p_{u}\;=\;-r^{2}\dot{r}\end{array}\right.

However, we will continue to assume arbitrary
n\neq 0
for now.
Before continuing to the dynamics, let us remark on some of the above features of choosing
m=-1
:

\,\scriptscriptstyle{\blacksquare}\,
Remark 3.1
.

—As seen from eq.(
3.1
), choosing
m=-1
leads to
r({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})=u^{n}
such that the radial distance,
r
, is a function only of
u
, but no longer a function of
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
. Thus, any function that depends only on
r
— such as a central force potential
V^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}(r)
— will also be a function only of
u
.
That is, choosing
m=-1
leads to
30
30

30

Compare to
m\neq-1
for which
r({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})=u^{n}|{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}|^{m+1}
and
\tfrac{\uppartial f(r)}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\neq{\vphantom{0}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{0}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
.

\displaystyle r({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})=u^{n}\hskip 18.49988pt,

\displaystyle\tfrac{\uppartial f(r)}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}={\vphantom{0}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{0}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\hskip 18.49988pt,

\displaystyle\tfrac{\uppartial f(r)}{\uppartial u}=\tfrac{\uppartial r}{\uppartial u}\tfrac{\uppartial f}{\uppartial r}=n\tfrac{u^{n}}{u}\tfrac{\uppartial f}{\uppartial r}

(332)

\,\scriptscriptstyle{\blacksquare}\,
Remark 3.2
.

—It was proven in section
2.4
that
q=|{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}|
and
\lambda({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
are integrals of motion and that we may choose these constants to be
q=1
and
\lambda=0
. This leads to the following two integrals of motion for our eight-dimensional phase space:

\displaystyle q\,=\,|{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}|\,=\,1

,

\displaystyle\lambda\,=\,\tfrac{1}{q^{2}}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,=\,0

(333)

The first is precisely our specified constraint,
\varphi({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})=0
, and holds for any
m
. However, only for
m=-1
does
\lambda=0
lead to the above relation that
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}

are orthogonal. All we must do to guarantee the above constants hold along all trajectories is specify initial conditions such that
q(t_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}})=1
and
\lambda(t_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}})=0
. That is, use eq.(
3.1
).

\,\scriptscriptstyle{\blacksquare}\,
Remark 3.3
.

—As seen from eq.(
3.1
), choosing
m=-1
leads to a phase space where
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
have the following properties:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}={\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\times{\hat{{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\;=\;{\hat{{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\\[6.0pt]
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}={\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\times{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;=\;r^{2}\dot{{\hat{{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\\[6.0pt]
{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}={\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\times{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\end{array}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=q^{2}=1\\[6.0pt]
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=p^{2}={\ell}^{2}\\[6.0pt]
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}={\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}={\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=0\end{array}

That is,
\{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\}
are mutually orthogonal with magnitudes
q=1
and
p={\ell}
.
Not only that, but
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
fully define the osculating Local Verticle, Local Horizontal (LVLH) orthonormal basis:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{cc}{\text{\footnotesize{osculating}}}\\
{\text{\footnotesize{LVLH basis}}}\end{array}\left\{\hskip 9.24994pt\begin{array}[]{ll}{\hat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\;=\;{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}&\;=\;{\text{\footnotesize{radial unit vector}}}\\[5.0pt]
{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\;=\;\tfrac{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{p}&\;=\;{\text{\footnotesize{tangent unit vector (NAME?)}}}\\[5.0pt]
{\hat{{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\;=\;{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\times{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}&\;=\;{\text{\footnotesize{orbit normal unit vector}}}\end{array}\right.

More specifically, the above are actually the
components
of the LVLH basis vectors in the inertial basis. However, if we define the “geometric, vectors”
{\textbf{{{q}}}}=q_{i}{{\mkern 1.5mu\hat{\mkern-1.5mu{\boldsymbol{\iota}}}}^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$$}}}}\vphantom{\iota}_{i}
and
{\textbf{{{p}}}}=p_{i}{{\mkern 1.5mu\hat{\mkern-1.5mu{\boldsymbol{\iota}}}}^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$$}}}}\vphantom{\iota}_{i}\,
(
i=1,2,3
), for orthonormal inertial basis vectors
{{\mkern 1.5mu\hat{\mkern-1.5mu{\boldsymbol{\iota}}}}^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$$}}}}\vphantom{\iota}_{i}
, then the above holds with
q
and
p
taking the place of
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
, respectively, and defines the geometric, coordinate-independent, LVLH basis vectors.

\,\scriptscriptstyle{\blacksquare}\,
Remark 3.4
.

For some known
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}

) and eq.(

). Note that we are free to simplify these using the constants

and
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=0
, leading to

31
31

31

eq.(
345
) should
not
be used to obtain the Hamiltonian, only to recover the position and velocity from some known
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}

\displaystyle{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;=\;u^{n}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\hskip 18.49988pt,\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;=\;\tfrac{1}{u^{n}}({\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;+\;\tfrac{1}{n}up_{u}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})

(345)

3.2  The Hamiltonian & Canonical Equations for Perturbed Central Force Motion

The Hamiltonian given by eq.(
322
) leads to the following canonical equations of motion:

\displaystyle{{H}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)\,=\,\tfrac{1}{u^{2n}}\tfrac{1}{2}\Big(q^{2}p^{2}-({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})^{2}\,+\,\tfrac{1}{n^{2}}u^{2}p_{u}^{2}\Big)\,+\,V^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}(u)\,+\,V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)

(346)

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}\dot{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{u^{2n}}\big[q^{2}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}-({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}){\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\big]\\[8.0pt]
\dot{u}\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial p_{u}}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{u^{2n}}\tfrac{1}{n^{2}}u^{2}p_{u}\\[8.0pt]
\dot{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,-\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,+\,{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,=\,-\tfrac{1}{u^{2n}}\big[p^{2}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}-({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}){\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\big]-\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,+\,{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\[8.0pt]
\dot{p}_{u}\,=\,-\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial u}\,+\,\alpha_{u}\,=\,\tfrac{n}{u^{2n+1}}\big(q^{2}p^{2}-({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})^{2}+\tfrac{n-1}{n^{3}}u^{2}p_{u}^{2})-\tfrac{\uppartial V^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}{\uppartial u}-\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial u}+\alpha_{u}\end{array}

where
{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
\alpha_{u}
are generalized accelerations arising from any non-conservative forces, as detailed in section
2.3
.
Now, as proven in section
2.4
and mentioned in Remark
3.2
, we may use the integrals of motion
q=1
and
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=\lambda=0

to simplify the above equations. This leads to

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}\dot{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{u^{2n}}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}&\quad,\\[8.0pt]
\dot{u}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{u^{2n}}\tfrac{1}{n^{2}}u^{2}p_{u}&\quad,\end{array}

\displaystyle\quad\begin{array}[]{ll}\dot{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,-\tfrac{1}{u^{2n}}p^{2}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}-\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}+{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\[8.0pt]
\dot{p}_{u}\,=\,\tfrac{n}{u^{2n+1}}\big(p^{2}+\tfrac{n-1}{n^{3}}u^{2}p_{u}^{2})-\tfrac{\uppartial V^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}{\uppartial u}-\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial u}+\alpha_{u}\end{array}

which are simplified considerably compared to what they would be for any
m\neq-1
, as given by eq.(
2.3
).
Note that the above equations are the same as what we would obtain if we had simplified the Hamiltonian using
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=0

before
differentiating.
Although we must be careful when simplifying the Hamiltonian in a non-minimal phase space, we can, in fact, discard the term
({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})^{2}
. The reason we can do this is because this term appears quadratically in
{{H}}
and thus all partials of this term will still contain
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=0
and likewise vanish.
Whether we use
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=0
before or after finding the equations of motion makes no difference in this case (this is
not
true for the constant
q=1
).

\,\scriptscriptstyle{\blacksquare}\,
Remark 3.5
.

By choosing
m=-1
such that
\lambda=\tfrac{1}{q^{2}}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=0
is an integral of motion, we are free to eliminate the term
({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})^{2}
from the Hamiltonian such that
{{H}}
is now given by

\displaystyle{{H}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)\;=\;\tfrac{1}{u^{2n}}\tfrac{1}{2}\big(q^{2}p^{2}\,+\,\tfrac{1}{n^{2}}u^{2}p_{u}^{2}\big)\,+\,V^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}(u)\,+\,V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)

(355)

where the fact that the central force potential is a function only of
u
, but not
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
, also holds only for
m=-1
.

The Perturbation Terms.

The only place the dominant central force appears in the equations of motion is through
\tfrac{\uppartial V^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}{\uppartial u}
in the equation for
\dot{p}_{u}
. As per Remark
3.1
,
V^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}
does not appear in the equation for
\dot{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}
.
The terms
\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}
and
\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial u}
contain all conservative perturbations. The generalized accelerations,
{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
\alpha_{u}
, contain all non-conservative perturbations.
As shown in sections
2.3
and
2.3
, these terms may be expressed in terms of the inertial components of the conservative and non-conservative perturbing forces (per unit mass) using eq.(
2.3
) and eq.(
2.3
). With
q=1
and
m=-1
these become
32
32

32

Note that
-\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=-\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial r}
and
{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=a^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}_{r}
are simply the radial components of the accelerations from conservative and non-conservative perturbations, respectively.

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rl}\begin{array}[]{cc}{\text{\footnotesize{conservative}}}\\
{\text{\footnotesize{perturbations}}}\end{array}&\left\{\quad\begin{array}[]{rll}\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}&\;=\;\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}&\;=\;u^{n}\big[\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,-\,(\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}){\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\big]\\[6.0pt]
\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial u}&\;=\;\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial u}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}&\;=\;nu^{n-1}\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\end{array}\right.\\[20.0pt]
\begin{array}[]{cc}{\text{\footnotesize{non-conservative}}}\\
{\text{\footnotesize{perturbations}}}\end{array}&\left\{\quad\;\begin{array}[]{rlll}{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}&\;=\;\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}&\;=\;u^{n}\big[{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}\;-\;({\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}){\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\big]\\[6.0pt]
\alpha_{u}&\;=\;\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial u}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}&\;=\;nu^{n-1}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\end{array}\right.\end{array}

where
-\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}
and
{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}
are the inertial components of the accelerations due to any conservative and non-conservative perturbations, respectively. If we define the inertial components of the
total
perturbing acceleration,

\displaystyle{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}\,:=\,-\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\;+\;{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}\;=\;\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}{\text{\footnotesize{inertial components of the {total}}}}\\
{\text{\footnotesize{acceleration from conservative and}}}\\
{\text{\footnotesize{non-conservative perturbations}}}\end{array}\right)

Then all of the perturbation terms appearing in the equations of motion may be combined and expressed in terms of
{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}
as

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lll}{\vphantom{f}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{f}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}:=\,-\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,+\,{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}&\;=\,u^{n}\big({\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}-{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle{\,\otimes\,}$}}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\big){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}\\[8.0pt]
f_{u}:=\,-\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial u}\,+\,\alpha_{u}&\;=\;n\tfrac{u^{n}}{u}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}\end{array}

Such that the equations of motion give by eq.(
3.2
) can be written as

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}\dot{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{u^{2n}}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}&\quad,\\[8.0pt]
\dot{u}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{u^{2n}}\tfrac{1}{n^{2}}u^{2}p_{u}&\quad,\end{array}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}\dot{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,-\tfrac{1}{u^{2n}}p^{2}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}+{\vphantom{f}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{f}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\[8.0pt]
\dot{p}_{u}\,=\,\tfrac{n}{u^{2n+1}}\big(p^{2}+\tfrac{n-1}{n^{3}}u^{2}p_{u}^{2})-\tfrac{\uppartial V^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}{\uppartial u}+f_{u}\end{array}

\,\scriptscriptstyle{\blacksquare}\,
Remark 3.6

(Angular Momentum)
.

From the relations
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\times{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}={\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
p={\ell}
from eq.(
3.3
), the equation of motion for the specific angular momentum is given in terms of the canonical coordinates and perturbing forces as

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}\dot{{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,\dot{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\times{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,+\,{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\times\dot{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\times{\vphantom{f}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{f}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;=\;u^{n}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\times{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}\\[6.0pt]
\dot{{\ell}}\;=\;\dot{p}\;=\;\dot{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\;=\;{\vphantom{f}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{f}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\;=\;u^{n}{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}\end{array}

which confirms that the angular momentum is constant for unperturbed central force motion and where the last equalities follow from eq.(
3.2
). Note that the first of the above is simply the classic equation
\dot{\bm{\ell}}={\textbf{{{r}}}}\times{\textbf{{{a}}}}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}
given in terms of cartesian components in the inertial basis.

3.3  Extended Phase Space & Transformation of the Evolution Parameter

So far, we have only developed the Hamiltonian which generates equations of motion with respect to time as given by eq.(
3.2
) or eq.(
3.2
). These equations are non-linear even in the case of unperturbed (
{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}={\vphantom{0}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{0}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
) motion, regardless of the form of the central force potential,
V^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}
. However, we can obtain equations that are fully linear in the unperturbed case if we transform the evolution parameter to something other than time. We will now consider a transformation of the evolution parameter from the time,
t
, to two new evolution parameters,
s
and
\tau
which we define through the following differential relations:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}&\tfrac{\mathrm{d}t}{\mathrm{d}s}\;=\;r^{2}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})\;=\;u^{2n}\,:=\,\acute{t}\\[6.0pt]
&\tfrac{\mathrm{d}t}{\mathrm{d}\tau}\;=\;\tfrac{r^{2}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})}{{\ell}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})}\;=\;\tfrac{u^{2n}}{\sqrt{q^{2}p^{2}-({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})^{2}}}\,:=\,\mathring{t}\end{array}

where a prime will denote differentiation with respect to
s
and a ring will denote differentiation with respect to
\tau
. Note that, for orbital motion,
\tau
corresponds to the true anomaly (up to some additive constant depending on how
\tau=0
is specified).
In the case of unperturbed motion (
{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}={\vphantom{0}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{0}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
) it is seen from eq.(
3.6
) that the angular momentum magnitude,
{\ell}=p
, is constant and the parameters
s
and
\tau
defined above are then related very simply by

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}\tau}{\mathrm{d}s}={\ell}=p

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\tau={\ell}s=ps\quad\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}{\text{\footnotesize{for unperturbed}}}\\
{\text{\footnotesize{motion, }}}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}={\vphantom{0}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{0}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\end{array}\right)

The Extended Phase Space.

As shown in section
B
,
we may transform the independent variable from time,
t
, to any other evolution parameter,
{\varepsilon}
, by making use of the
extended phase space
where time is treated like an additional generalized coordinate with conjugate momenta
p_{t}
. Given some differential relation
\tfrac{\mathrm{d}t}{\mathrm{d}{\varepsilon}}
— which is some function of the canonical coordinates as in eq.(
3.3
) — the
extended Hamiltonian
, denoted
\widetilde{{H}}
, is then given by eq.(
963
) in section
B
as

extended Hamiltonian:

\displaystyle\quad\widetilde{{H}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t,p_{t})\;=\;\tfrac{\mathrm{d}t}{\mathrm{d}{\varepsilon}}({{H}}+p_{t})

(379)

As seen in eq.(
B
) of section
B
, the canonical equations of motion with
{\varepsilon}
as the evolution parameter are then found from
\widetilde{{H}}
in a familiar manner:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}&\tfrac{\mathrm{d}{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}{\mathrm{d}{\varepsilon}}\;=\;\tfrac{\uppartial\widetilde{{H}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\\[6.0pt]
&\tfrac{\mathrm{d}t}{\mathrm{d}{\varepsilon}}\;=\;\tfrac{\uppartial\widetilde{{H}}}{\uppartial p_{t}}\end{array}\qquad\qquad\begin{array}[]{ll}&\tfrac{\mathrm{d}{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}{\mathrm{d}{\varepsilon}}\;=\;-\tfrac{\uppartial\widetilde{{H}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\;+\;\tfrac{\mathrm{d}t}{\mathrm{d}{\varepsilon}}{\bar{{\vphantom{\alpha}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alpha}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\\[6.0pt]
&\tfrac{\mathrm{d}p_{t}}{\mathrm{d}{\varepsilon}}\;=\;-\tfrac{\uppartial\widetilde{{H}}}{\uppartial t}\;+\;\tfrac{\mathrm{d}t}{\mathrm{d}{\varepsilon}}\alpha_{t}\end{array}

Where
{\bar{{\vphantom{\alpha}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alpha}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}=({\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},\alpha_{u})
are the same generalized accelerations given in eq.(
3.2
) and where
\alpha_{t}
has units of power (per unit reduced mass) and is given by eq.(
B
) in section
B
as

\displaystyle\alpha_{t}

\displaystyle\;=\;-{\bar{{\vphantom{\alpha}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alpha}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\dot{{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\;=\;-{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\dot{{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\;=\;-{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\dot{{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\;=\;-{\textbf{{{a}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\dot{{\textbf{{{r}}}}}

(384)

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}t}{\mathrm{d}{\varepsilon}}\alpha_{t}

\displaystyle\;=\;-{\bar{{\vphantom{\alpha}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alpha}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\tfrac{\mathrm{d}{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}{\mathrm{d}{\varepsilon}}\;=\;-{\textbf{{{a}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\tfrac{\mathrm{d}{\textbf{{{r}}}}}{\mathrm{d}{\varepsilon}}

where we have used the definition of
{\bar{{\vphantom{\alpha}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alpha}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
given in eq.(
297
). Thus, for the problem at hand,
\alpha_{t}
is the negative of the rate that the non-conservative force does work on the system (per unit mass).
33
33

33

In general, this is not always true because it is not always true that
\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\dot{{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}=\dot{{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}
. However, this does hold for the case that
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
) is not an explicit function of time, as is currently the case.

Furthermore, as seen in eq.(
962
) of section
B
,
p_{t}
is related the the
value
of the non-extended Hamiltonian by
p_{t}=-{{H}}
.
34
34

34

For the problem at hand, this means that
p_{t}
is equal to the negative of the total energy (per unit mass) of the particle.

Thus, the canonical coordinates in the extended phase space are related by:

\displaystyle p_{t}\;=\;-\tfrac{1}{u^{2n}}\tfrac{1}{2}\big(p^{2}+\tfrac{1}{n^{2}}u^{2}p_{u}^{2}\big)\,-\,V^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\,-\,V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}

,

\displaystyle\widetilde{{H}}\,=\,0

(385)

In this way, the extended phase space is itself a sort of non-minimal phase space subject to the above “constraint”.
It is important to note that, when differentiating the extended Hamiltonian,
\widetilde{{H}}
,
p_{t}
must be treated as an independent phase space variable,
not
as a function of the other coordinates and momenta as the above relation would imply. However, after obtaining the equations of motion, the above relation may be substituted into the equations of motion themselves (although this is not necessary).

The Extended Phase Space for the Parameter
s
.

With
\tfrac{\mathrm{d}t}{\mathrm{d}s}
given in eq.(
3.3
),
the extended Hamiltonian for the parameter
s
is found from eq.(
355
) and
eq.(
379
) as

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lr}\widetilde{{H}}\,=\,\frac{1}{2}\Big(q^{2}p^{2}+\tfrac{1}{n^{2}}u^{2}p_{u}^{2}\Big)\,+\,\widetilde{V}^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}(u)\,+\,\widetilde{V}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)\,+\,u^{2n}p_{t}\;=\;0\end{array}

where the augmented potentials are defined as
35
35

35

As per Remark
3.1
and eq.(
332
), the fact that
\widetilde{V}^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}(u)
is still a function only of
u
, but not
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
, holds only for the the point transformation of the form
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})=u^{n}{\hat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\,
(

{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})=u^{n}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,
(
m=0
), then we would have
\acute{t}=r^{2}=u^{2n}|{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}|^{2}
and
\widetilde{V}^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
.

\displaystyle\widetilde{V}^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}(u)\,=\,u^{2n}V^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}(u)\qquad,\qquad\widetilde{V}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)\;=\;u^{2n}V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},t)

, respectively:

\displaystyle\hbox to0.0pt{$\;\,\acute{\vphantom{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\!\acute{\vphantom{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}$\hss}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;=\;-{\ell}^{2}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\qquad,

\displaystyle{\hbox to0.0pt{$\,\mathring{\vphantom{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\mkern 4.5mu\mathring{\vphantom{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}$\hss}}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;=\;-{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}

(414)

Solution for
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{s}
and
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{s}
.

For convenience, let
s_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}=0
. The closed-form solution to the first equation in eq.(
414
) is then readily found as:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rl}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{s}&={\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos{\ell}s+\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}\acute{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\sin{\ell}s\\[6.0pt]
&={\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos{\ell}s+\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\sin{\ell}s\end{array}

,

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rl}\acute{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}_{s}&=-{\ell}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\sin{\ell}s+\acute{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos{\ell}s\\[6.0pt]
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{s}&=-{\ell}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\sin{\ell}s+{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos{\ell}s\end{array}

where
(\;)_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}=(\;)|_{s_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}}
are initial conditions and where the second line has made use of the fact that
\acute{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}={\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
from eq.(
3.3
).

Solution for
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{\tau}
and
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{\tau}
.

The solutions in terms of
\tau
may be found by solving the trivial equation seen in eq.(
414
) or by using the above solution in terms of
s
along with the simple relations
\mathring{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}=\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}\acute{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}=\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}={\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
and the fact that
\tau={\ell}s
for unperturbed motion as seen in eq.(
3.3
). Either way, the result is

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rl}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{\tau}&={\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos\tau+\mathring{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\sin\tau\\[6.0pt]
&={\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos\tau+{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\sin\tau\end{array}

,

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rl}\mathring{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}_{\tau}&=-{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\sin\tau+\mathring{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos\tau\\[6.0pt]
{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}_{\tau}&=-{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\sin\tau+{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos\tau\end{array}

Where the second line has made use of the fact that
\mathring{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}={\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
from eq.(
3.3
).

\,\scriptscriptstyle{\blacksquare}\,
Remark 3.7
.

—For the closed-form solutions for
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
in eq.(
3.4
) and eq.(
3.4
), note the following:

•

The solutions are valid for arbitrary central forces of any form.

•

It was assumed that
s_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}=\tau_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}=0
simply for convenience. The solutions are equally valid with
s
and
\tau
replaced by
\Delta s=s-s_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
and
\Delta\tau=\tau-\tau_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
, respectively, for arbitrary
s_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
and
\tau_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
. Thus, in the case of orbital motion, there is no need to locate periapse.

•

The solutions are independent of the radial coordinate,
u=r^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{n}}}
, and are valid for any value of
n
in the point transformation
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})=u^{n}{\hat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
of eq.(
3.1
).

Linear Equations for
u
as a Perturbed Oscillator

Obtaining linear equations for
u
is not quite as simple as it was for
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
. Unlike the equations of motion for
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
, the equation of motion for
u
does depend on the central force potential,
V^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}
.
From eq.(
3.3
) and eq.(
3.3
) , we see that

\displaystyle\hbox to0.0pt{$\;\,\acute{\vphantom{u}}\!\acute{\vphantom{u}}$\hss}u\;=\;\tfrac{2}{n^{2}}u\acute{u}p_{u}\,+\,\tfrac{1}{n^{2}}u^{2}\acute{p}_{u}\;=\;\tfrac{2}{n^{4}}u^{3}p_{u}^{2}\,+\,\tfrac{1}{n^{2}}u^{2}\acute{p}_{u}

(423)

\displaystyle{\hbox to0.0pt{$\,\mathring{\vphantom{u}}\mkern 4.5mu\mathring{\vphantom{u}}$\hss}}u\;=\;\tfrac{2}{pn^{2}}u\mathring{u}p_{u}\,+\,\tfrac{u^{2}}{pn^{2}}\mathring{p}_{u}\,-\,\tfrac{u^{2}p_{u}}{n^{2}}\tfrac{\mathring{p}}{p^{2}}\;=\;\tfrac{2u^{3}}{p^{2}n^{4}}p_{u}^{2}\,+\,\tfrac{u^{2}}{pn^{2}}\mathring{p}_{u}\,-\,\tfrac{u^{2}p_{u}}{n^{2}}\tfrac{\mathring{p}}{p^{2}}

(424)

Substitution of
\acute{p}_{u}
from eq.(
3.3
),
\mathring{p}_{u}
from eq.(
406
), and
\mathring{p}
from eq.(
407
) into the above leads to

38
38

38

these relations may also be found from one another using
\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}=\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}
and
\tfrac{\mathrm{d}^{2}{}}{\mathrm{d}{\tau}^{2}}=\tfrac{1}{{\ell}^{2}}(\tfrac{\mathrm{d}^{2}{}}{\mathrm{d}{s}^{2}}-\tfrac{\acute{{\ell}}}{{\ell}}\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s})
.

\displaystyle\hbox to0.0pt{$\;\,\acute{\vphantom{u}}\!\acute{\vphantom{u}}$\hss}u

\displaystyle\;=\;\tfrac{u}{n}\big({\ell}^{2}\,+\,\tfrac{1+n}{n^{3}}u^{2}p_{u}^{2}\,-\,\tfrac{1}{n}u^{2n+1}\tfrac{\uppartial V^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}{\uppartial u}\big)\,+\,\tfrac{u^{2n+2}}{n^{2}}(-\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial u}+\alpha_{u})

(425)

\displaystyle{\hbox to0.0pt{$\,\mathring{\vphantom{u}}\mkern 4.5mu\mathring{\vphantom{u}}$\hss}}u

\displaystyle\;=\;\tfrac{u}{n}\big(1+\tfrac{1+n}{n^{3}}\tfrac{u^{2}p_{u}^{2}}{{\ell}^{2}}-\tfrac{u^{2n+1}}{n{\ell}^{2}}\tfrac{\uppartial V^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}{\uppartial u}\big)\;+\;\tfrac{u^{2n}}{{\ell}^{2}}\big[\tfrac{u^{2}}{n^{2}}(-\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial u}+\alpha_{u})-\mathring{u}(-\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}+{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\big]

(426)

where we have again written
{\ell}
instead of
p
for the angular momentum magnitude which is constant for unperturbed motion.
Looking at eq.(
425
) and eq.(
426
), we note that neither is the equation of a perturbed oscillator, regardless of the central force potential
V^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}
. The first term in parenthesis — those not involving the perturbation terms — is not, in general, constant for unperturbed motion on account of
p_{u}
, which is related to
\dot{r}
as in eq.(
3.1
). In order for these equations to be linear in the case of unperturbed motion requires the elimination of the
p_{u}
term. This requires
n=-1
.

Second-Order Oscillator Equations for
u=1/r
(i.e.,
n=–1
)

Let us consider
\hbox to0.0pt{$\;\,\acute{\vphantom{u}}\!\acute{\vphantom{u}}$\hss}u
and
{\hbox to0.0pt{$\,\mathring{\vphantom{u}}\mkern 4.5mu\mathring{\vphantom{u}}$\hss}}u
for the case that
n=-1
such that
u=\tfrac{1}{r}
. eq.(
425
) and eq.(
426
) then simplify to

\displaystyle\text{\small{$n=-1$}}\;\;\left\{\;\;\begin{array}[]{rl}\hbox to0.0pt{$\;\,\acute{\vphantom{u}}\!\acute{\vphantom{u}}$\hss}u+\big({\ell}^{2}\,+\,\tfrac{1}{u}\tfrac{\uppartial V^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}{\uppartial u}\big)u&\,=\,(-\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial u}+\alpha_{u})\;\;=\;\;-\tfrac{1}{u^{2}}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\[14.0pt]
{\hbox to0.0pt{$\,\mathring{\vphantom{u}}\mkern 4.5mu\mathring{\vphantom{u}}$\hss}}u+\big(1+\tfrac{1}{u{\ell}^{2}}\tfrac{\uppartial V^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}{\uppartial u}\big)u&\,=\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}^{2}}\big[(-\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial u}+\alpha_{u})-\tfrac{\mathring{u}}{u^{2}}(-\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}+{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\big]\\[4.0pt]
&\,=\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}^{2}u^{3}}\big[({\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})u+({\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})\mathring{u}\big]\end{array}\right.

Where we have used eq.(
3.2
) to express the perturbations in terms of
{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}
(the total perturbing force per unit mass). Looking at the above, it can be seen that these equations simplify to those of a perturbed oscillator for the following cases of the central potential,
V^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}(u)
:

39
39

39

The force (per unit mass),
-\nabla_{\hskip-1.70717pt{\,}}V^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}
, associated with the potentials in eq.(
3.4
) are given by
-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{1}}{r^{2}}{\mkern 1.5mu\hat{\mkern-1.5mu{\textbf{{{r}}}}}}
for case 1 (e.g., the Kepler problem) and
-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{2}}{r^{3}}{\mkern 1.5mu\hat{\mkern-1.5mu{\textbf{{{r}}}}}}
for case 2. Case 3 is simply the superposition of case 1 and case 2.

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}\text{case 1 (Kepler)}\\[4.0pt]
V^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}(u)=-{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{1}u\\[6.0pt]
V^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}(r)=-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{1}}{r}\end{array}\quad\left|\quad\begin{array}[]{ll}\text{case 2}\\[4.0pt]
V^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}(u)=-\tfrac{1}{2}{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{2}u^{2}\\[6.0pt]
V^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}(r)=-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{2}}{2r^{2}}\end{array}\right.\quad\left|\quad\begin{array}[]{ll}\text{case 3}\\[4.0pt]
V^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}(u)=-{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{1}u-\tfrac{1}{2}{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{2}u^{2}\\[6.0pt]
V^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}(r)=-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{1}}{r}-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{2}}{2r^{2}}\end{array}\right.

where
{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{1}
and
{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{2}
are related to some physical constants (e.g., gravitational parameter, spring stiffness, Coulomb constant, etc.). We have made no restrictions as to weather
{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{1}
and
{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{2}
are positive or negative. The negative signs seen above are simply included such that the forces (per unit mass) resulting from the above potentials are attractive — directed towards the origin at
{\textbf{{{r}}}}={\textbf{{{0}}}}
— if
{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{1}>0
and
{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{2}>0
and are repulsive if
{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{1}<0
and
{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{2}<0
.
we shall consider only case 3 for the remainder this section as it is a superposition of both/either of first two and therefore the most general. For this case, eq.(
3.4
) leads to:

\displaystyle\text{\small{$\begin{array}[]{cc}\text{for }n=-1\text{ and,}\\[3.0pt]
V^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=-{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.09999pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{1}u-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.16998pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{2}u^{2}}{2}\end{array}$}}\;\;\left\{\;\;\begin{array}[]{ll}\hbox to0.0pt{$\;\,\acute{\vphantom{u}}\!\acute{\vphantom{u}}$\hss}u\,+\,({\ell}^{2}-{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{2})u\,-\,{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{1}\,=\,-\tfrac{1}{u^{2}}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\[8.0pt]
{\hbox to0.0pt{$\,\mathring{\vphantom{u}}\mkern 4.5mu\mathring{\vphantom{u}}$\hss}}u\,+\,(1-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{2}}{{\ell}^{2}})u-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}=\tfrac{1}{{\ell}^{2}u^{3}}\big[({\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})u+({\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})\mathring{u}\big]\end{array}\right.

The equations for case 1 and case 2 are then found simply by setting
{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{2}=0
and
{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{1}=0
, respectively. The above are the equations of a perturbed oscillator. For unperturbed motion, the right-hand-side of the above vanishes and
{\ell}
is constant such that, for both of the above equations, the coefficient of
u
and the term involving
{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{1}
are constants. For general perturbed motion,
{\ell}
is no longer constant but evolves according to eq.(
413
) (now with
n=-1
).

Unperturbed Solution for
u
and
p_{u}
(for
n=–1
)

We have seen that, if we choose
n=-1
such that
u=\tfrac{1}{r}
and
p_{u}=-r^{2}\dot{r}
, then the unperturbed equations for
\hbox to0.0pt{$\;\,\acute{\vphantom{u}}\!\acute{\vphantom{u}}$\hss}u
and
{\hbox to0.0pt{$\,\mathring{\vphantom{u}}\mkern 4.5mu\mathring{\vphantom{u}}$\hss}}u
are linear in
u
only if the the central force potential,
V^{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}
, has one of the general forms seen in eq.(
3.4
). For the most general case (case 3), the unperturbed equations of motion are given by eq.(
3.4
) with the right-hand-side set to zero:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rllll}\hbox to0.0pt{$\;\,\acute{\vphantom{u}}\!\acute{\vphantom{u}}$\hss}u\,+\,\omega^{2}u\,-\,{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{1}&\;=\;0&\qquad,&\qquad\omega^{2}\,:=\,{\ell}^{2}-{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{2}\\[10.0pt]
{\hbox to0.0pt{$\,\mathring{\vphantom{u}}\mkern 4.5mu\mathring{\vphantom{u}}$\hss}}u\,+\,\varpi^{2}u\,-\,\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{1}}{{\ell}^{2}}&\;=\;0&\qquad,&\qquad\varpi^{2}\,:=\,1-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{2}}{{\ell}^{2}}\,=\,\omega^{2}/{\ell}^{2}\end{array}

If
{\ell}^{2}-{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{2}>0
then the above are the equations for an oscillator with a constant driving “force”. Note that the frequencies
\omega
and
\varpi
defined above are related simply by

\displaystyle\varpi{\ell}\;=\;\omega\qquad,

\displaystyle\varpi\tau\;=\;\omega s

(470)

where the second of the above follows from the fact that
\tau={\ell}s
for unperturbed motion as seen in eq.(
3.3
). For the Kepler problem,
{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{2}=0
and thus
\omega={\ell}
and
\varpi=1
.

Unperturbed Solution for
u_{s}
and
p_{u_{s}}
.

The solution to the first equation in eq.(
3.4
) is easily found in terms of initial conditions as

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}u_{s}\,=\,(u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{1}}{\omega^{2}})\cos\omega s\,+\,\tfrac{1}{\omega}\acute{u}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\sin{\omega s}\,+\,\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{1}}{\omega^{2}}\\[8.0pt]
\acute{u}_{s}\,=\,-\omega(u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{1}}{\omega^{2}})\sin\omega s\,+\,\acute{u}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos\omega s\end{array}

where we have again assumed
s_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}=0
simply for convenience. From eq.(
3.3
) with
n=-1
, we see that
p_{u}=\tfrac{\acute{u}}{u^{2}}
. The above then leads to the solutions for
u_{s}
and
p_{u_{s}}
as

\displaystyle u_{s}\,=\,(u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{1}}{\omega^{2}})\cos\omega s\,+\,\tfrac{1}{\omega}u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}^{2}p_{u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}}\sin{\omega s}\,+\,\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{1}}{\omega^{2}}

\displaystyle p_{u_{s}}\;=\;\frac{-\omega(u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{1}}{\omega^{2}})\sin\omega s\,+\,u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}^{2}p_{u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}}\cos\omega s}{\big[(u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{1}}{\omega^{2}})\cos\omega s\,+\,\tfrac{1}{\omega}u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}^{2}p_{u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}}\sin{\omega s}\,+\,\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{1}}{\omega^{2}}\big]^{2}}

Unperturbed Solution for
u_{\tau}
and
p_{u_{\tau}}
.

The solution to the second equation in eq.(
3.4
) is easily found in terms of initial conditions as

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}u_{\tau}\,=\,(u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{1}}{\omega^{2}})\cos\varpi\tau\,+\,\tfrac{1}{\varpi}\mathring{u}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\sin{\varpi\tau}\,+\,\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{1}}{\omega^{2}}\\[8.0pt]
\mathring{u}_{\tau}\,=\,-\varpi(u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}_{1}}{\omega^{2}})\sin\varpi\tau\,+\,\mathring{u}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos\varpi\tau\end{array}

where we have used the relation
\varpi{\ell}=\omega
.
Using
