u_{s}&\,=\,(u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}})\cos{\ell}s\,+\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}w_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\sin{{\ell}s}\,+\,\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}}\\[6.0pt]
w_{s}&\,=\,-{\ell}(u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}})\sin{\ell}s\,+\,w_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos{\ell}s&\quad=\acute{u}\end{array}\right.

where
{\ell}={\ell}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}=p_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}={\lvert\acute{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\rvert}
for Kepler motion.
And for
{\varepsilon}=\tau
:

\displaystyle{\vphantom{{{\text{\textchi}}}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{{\text{\textchi}}}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\vphantom{{{\text{\textchi}}}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{{\text{\textchi}}}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}},\tau)\;\;\left\{\;\;\begin{array}[]{rlll}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{\tau}&\,=\,{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos\tau\,+\,{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\sin\tau\\[6.0pt]
{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}_{\tau}&\,=\,-{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\sin\tau\,+\,{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos\tau&\quad\;=\,\mathring{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\\[6.0pt]
u_{\tau}&\,=\,(u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}})\cos\tau\,+\,w^{\tau}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\sin{\tau}\,+\,\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}}\\[6.0pt]
\tilde{w}_{\tau}&\,=\,-(u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}})\sin\tau\,+\,w^{\tau}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos\tau&\quad\;=\,\mathring{u}\end{array}\right.

Note that eq.(
4.2.2
) and eq.(
4.2.2
) can both be written in terms of either
s
or
\tau
using eq.(
3.3
):

\displaystyle\tau\;=\;{\ell}s\qquad\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}{\text{\footnotesize{for unperturbed}}}\\
{\text{\footnotesize{motion, }}}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}={\vphantom{0}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{0}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\end{array}\right)

Phase Space Solution for
({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
.

The solutions for
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
,
u
, and
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
are already given in eq.(
4.2.2
) and eq.(
4.2.2
). The solution for
p_{u}
is easily obtained from
p_{u}=\tfrac{w}{u^{2}}
or
p_{u}={\ell}\tfrac{\tilde{w}}{u^{2}}
. Either way, we have:

\displaystyle{\vphantom{z}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{z}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\vphantom{z}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{z}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}},\tau)\;\;\left\{\;\;\begin{array}[]{rll}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{\tau}&\,=\,{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos\tau\,+\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\sin\tau\\[6.0pt]
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{\tau}&\,=\,-{\ell}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\sin\tau\,+\,{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos\tau\\[6.0pt]
u_{\tau}&\,=\,(u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}})\cos\tau\,+\,\tfrac{u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}^{2}}{{\ell}}p_{u|0}\sin{\tau}\,+\,\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}}\\[8.0pt]
p_{u|\tau}&\;=\;\dfrac{-{\ell}(u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}})\sin\tau\,+\,u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}^{2}p_{u|0}\cos\tau}{\big[(u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}})\cos\tau\,+\,\tfrac{u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}^{2}}{{\ell}}p_{u|0}\sin{\tau}\,+\,\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}}\big]^{2}}\end{array}\right.

And the solution in terms of
s
is easily obtained using eq.(
4.2.2
).

Solutions for
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
.

We can use eq.(
609
) or eq.(
4.2
) to recover the closed-form solutions for
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
(cartesian position and velocity components in the inertial basis). After substitution of the above solutions for
{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
and
{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rl}{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{\tau}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{u}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}&\,=\,\dfrac{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos\tau\,+\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\sin\tau}{(u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}})\cos\tau\,+\,\tfrac{u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}^{2}}{{\ell}}p_{u|0}\sin{\tau}\,+\,\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}}}\\[24.0pt]
{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{\tau}\,=\,u{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}-u^{2}p_{u}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}&\,=\,\big[u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}+\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}}(\cos\tau-1)\big]{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\,-\,(u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}^{2}p_{u|0}+\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}}\sin\tau){\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\\[6.0pt]
&\,=\,{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\,+\,\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}}\big[{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}(\cos\tau-1)\,-\,{\ell}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\sin{\tau}\big]\\[6.0pt]
&\,=\,{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\,+\,\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}}\big({\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{\tau}-{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\big)\end{array}

where
{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}:=u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}^{2}p_{u|0}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}=u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-w_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}

and, as usual, the solutions in terms of
s
are obtained using
\tau={\ell}s
.

Solutions for
t(s)
and
t(\tau)

The time,
t
, is related to the parameters
s
and
\tau
by the differential relations given in eq.(
662
). It will be easiest to first find to solution for
t(\tau)
. From eq.(
662
) and eq.(
4.2.2
), we obtain

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}t}{\mathrm{d}\tau}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}u^{2}}

,

\displaystyle t-t_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}\int_{\tau_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}}^{\tau}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}\tau}{\big[(u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}})\cos\tau\,+\,\tfrac{u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}^{2}}{{\ell}}p_{u|0}\sin{\tau}\,+\,\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}}\big]^{2}}

\,\scriptscriptstyle{\blacksquare}\,
Remark 4.3
.

eq.(
4.2.2
) through eq.(
4.2.2
) give the closed-form Kepler solutions for Lagrangian coordinates,
{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},\hbox to0.0pt{$\;\,\acute{\vphantom{{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}}\!\acute{\vphantom{{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}}$\hss}{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
and
{\vphantom{{{\text{\textchi}}}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{{\text{\textchi}}}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},\mathring{{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}})
, and canonical coordinates,
{\vphantom{z}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{z}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
. Note the following:

•

It was assumed that
s_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}=\tau_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}=0
simply for convenience. The solutions are equally valid with
s
and
\tau
replaced by
\Delta s=s-s_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
and
\Delta\tau=\tau-\tau_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
, respectively, for some arbitrary
s_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
and
\tau_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
. Thus, there is no need to locate periapse.

•

The specific angular momentum is constant for unperturbed Keplerian motion and is given in terms of the canonical coordinates by
{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}={\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\times{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\ell}=p=|\acute{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}|
.

•

For unperturbed motion, the evolution parameters
s
and
\tau
(the true anomaly up to an additive constant) are related simply by eq.(
4.2.2
):
{\ell}s=\tau
. Thus, any function
f(\tau)
is easily written in terms of
s
, and vice versa, using

\displaystyle f(s)=\left.{f(\tau)}\right|_{\tau={\ell}s}\hskip 18.49988pt\leftrightarrow\hskip 18.49988ptf(\tau)=\left.{f(s)}\right|_{s=\tau/{\ell}}

(752)

State Transition Matrices

For any
n
-dim linear inhomogeneous ODE,
\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}{\varepsilon}}{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=A{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}+{\vphantom{b}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{b}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
(with
A\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n\times n}
and
b\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
constant), the solution may be expressed in terms state transition matrix (STM),
\Phi_{{\varepsilon}}
, as:

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}{\varepsilon}}{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,=\,A{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}+{\vphantom{b}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{b}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\hskip 9.24994pt,\hskip 9.24994pt{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}(0)={\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 9.24994pt\Rightarrow\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 9.24994pt{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{\varepsilon}}\,=\,\Phi_{{\varepsilon}}{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}+{\vphantom{y}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{y}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{\varepsilon}

(753)

where
\Phi_{{\varepsilon}}={\mathrm{e}}^{tA}\in\mathrm{Gl}^{n}_{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathbb{R}}}}
is the STM for the solution to the homogeneous ODE,
\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}{\varepsilon}}{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=A{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
, and where
{\vphantom{y}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{y}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{\varepsilon}}=\int_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}^{{\varepsilon}}\Phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}_{{\varepsilon}}{\vphantom{b}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{b}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mathrm{d}}{\varepsilon}
(where
\Phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}_{{\varepsilon}}=\Phi_{-{\varepsilon}}
). Note that
{\vphantom{y}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{y}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{\varepsilon}
does not depend on
{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and thus the relation
\Phi_{{\varepsilon}}=\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{\varepsilon}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{x}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{x}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}}
gives the same STM in the case
{\vphantom{b}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{b}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=0
or
{\vphantom{b}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{b}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\neq 0
.

STM for Lagrangian Coordinates,
{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
.

The STM for the Lagrangian coordinates
{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},u,{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},w)
defined in eq.(
4.2.2
) is given by the Jacobian
\Phi_{{\varepsilon}}=\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{\varepsilon}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}}

(where either
{\varepsilon}=s
or
{\varepsilon}=\tau
).
This is easily obtained from the Kepler solution — treating
{\ell}={\lvert{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\rvert}
as a
constant
(
\tfrac{\uppartial\,{\ell}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}}=0
) — given by eq.(
4.2.2
):

42
42

42

Similarly, the STM for
{\vphantom{{{\text{\textchi}}}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{{\text{\textchi}}}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},u,{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},\tilde{w})
defined in eq.(
4.2.2
) is obtained from eq.(
4.2.2
) as:

\displaystyle\Lambda_{{\varepsilon}}=\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{{{\text{\textchi}}}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{{\text{\textchi}}}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{\varepsilon}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{{{\text{\textchi}}}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{{\text{\textchi}}}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}}=\begin{pmatrix}{\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{4}\cos\omega{\varepsilon}&\;\;{\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{4}\sin\omega{\varepsilon}\\[5.0pt]
-{\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{4}\sin\omega{\varepsilon}&\;\;{\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{4}\cos\omega{\varepsilon}\end{pmatrix}

,

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}\,=\,\Lambda(-{\varepsilon})\,=\,\Lambda^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}

(754)

which has an even simpler form than the STM for
{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
in eq.(
755
).
However, as per Remark
4.2
,
{\vphantom{{{\text{\textchi}}}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{{\text{\textchi}}}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
is not truly a complete state vector and we shall give it no further consideration.

\displaystyle\Phi_{{\varepsilon}}=\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{\varepsilon}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}}=\begin{pmatrix}{\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{4}\cos\omega{\varepsilon}&\;\;{\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{4}\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}\sin\omega{\varepsilon}\\[5.0pt]
-{\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{4}{\ell}\sin\omega{\varepsilon}&\;\;{\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{4}\cos\omega{\varepsilon}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathrm{Sp}^{8}_{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathbb{R}}}}

\displaystyle\quad\Phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}_{{\varepsilon}}=\Phi_{-{\varepsilon}}

(755)

Where, from eq.(
4.2.2
), the above may be considered as a function of either
s
or
\tau
:

\displaystyle\omega{\varepsilon}={\ell}s=\tau

(756)

For Keplerian motion, the STM may be used to propagate the state as:

\displaystyle{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{\varepsilon}}\;=\;\Phi_{{\varepsilon}}{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\;+\;{\vphantom{y}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{y}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle{\vphantom{y}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{y}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{\varepsilon}}\,=\,\begin{pmatrix}{\vphantom{0}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{0}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\
\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}}(1-\cos\omega{\varepsilon})\\
{\vphantom{0}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{0}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\
\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}}\sin\omega{\varepsilon}\end{pmatrix}

(757)

which agrees with the Kepler solution given in eq.(
4.2.2
).
We may invert eq.(
757
) in order to express
{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
in terms of
{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{\varepsilon}}
as

\displaystyle{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\;=\;\Phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}_{{\varepsilon}}{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{\varepsilon}}\,-\,{\vphantom{Y}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Y}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle{\vphantom{Y}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Y}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{\varepsilon}}\;=\;\Phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}_{{\varepsilon}}{\vphantom{y}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{y}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{\varepsilon}}\,=\,\begin{pmatrix}{\vphantom{0}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{0}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\
-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}}(1-\cos\omega{\varepsilon})\\
{\vphantom{0}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{0}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\
\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}}\sin\omega{\varepsilon}\end{pmatrix}

(758)

Note that, regardless of whether the solutions are expressed in terms of
s
or
\tau
, both
\Phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}
and
{\vphantom{Y}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Y}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
depend on
{\ell}
which is constant only for Keplerian motion. For general perturbed motion, we must consider
{\ell}=p
as a function of
{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
, such that (suppressing
{\varepsilon}
):

\displaystyle\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}

\displaystyle\;=\;\tfrac{\uppartial}{\uppartial{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\big(\Phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,-\,{\vphantom{Y}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Y}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\big)\;=\;\Phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}+\,(\tfrac{\uppartial\Phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}}{\uppartial{\ell}}{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})\otimes\tfrac{\uppartial\,{\ell}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,-\,\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{Y}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Y}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\ell}}\otimes\tfrac{\uppartial\,{\ell}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\quad\neq\,\Phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}

(759)

the partials of
\Phi^{\scriptscriptstyle{-\!1}}
and
{\vphantom{Y}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Y}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
with respect to
{\ell}
are considerably shortened if we use
\tau
as the evolution parameter as this eliminates
{\ell}
from the argument of
\sin\omega{\varepsilon}
and
\cos\omega{\varepsilon}
.

STM for Phase Space Coordinates,
{\vphantom{z}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{z}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
.

Let
\Psi=\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{z}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{z}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{\varepsilon}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{z}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{z}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}}
denote the STM for the canonical coordinates,
{\vphantom{z}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{z}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
. We could calculate
\Psi
by directly differentiating the Kepler solution for
{\vphantom{z}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{z}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
given by eq.(
4.2.2
).
However, we can also use the fact that
\Psi
is related to the above
\Phi
through

\displaystyle\Psi_{{\varepsilon}}:=\,\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{z}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{z}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{\varepsilon}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{z}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{z}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}}

\displaystyle=\;\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{z}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{z}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{\varepsilon}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{\varepsilon}}}\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{\varepsilon}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}}\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{z}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{z}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}}=\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{z}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{z}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{\varepsilon}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{\varepsilon}}}\Phi_{{\varepsilon}}\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{z}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{z}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}}

(760)

where
{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{z}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{z}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
differ only in their eighth coordinates which are related by
w=u^{2}p_{u}
such that
\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{z}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{z}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}
and
\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{z}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{z}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}
are given (in terms of
{\vphantom{z}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{z}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
) by

\displaystyle\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{z}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{z}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\;=\;\begin{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\;{\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{4}\;\;\end{pmatrix}&\begin{pmatrix}\;{O}_{4}\;\;\end{pmatrix}\\[6.0pt]
\begin{pmatrix}{O}_{3}&{\vphantom{0}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{0}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\[4.0pt]
{\vphantom{0}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{0}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}&2up_{u}\end{pmatrix}&\begin{pmatrix}{\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}&{\vphantom{0}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{0}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\[4.0pt]
{\vphantom{0}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{0}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}&u^{2}\end{pmatrix}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{z}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{z}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\;=\;\begin{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\;{\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{4}\;\;\end{pmatrix}&\begin{pmatrix}\;{O}_{4}\;\;\end{pmatrix}\\[6.0pt]
\begin{pmatrix}{O}_{3}&{\vphantom{0}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{0}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\[4.0pt]
{\vphantom{0}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{0}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}&-2\tfrac{p_{u}}{u^{3}}\end{pmatrix}&\begin{pmatrix}{\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}&{\vphantom{0}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{0}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\[4.0pt]
{\vphantom{0}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{0}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}&\tfrac{1}{u^{2}}\end{pmatrix}\end{pmatrix}

(761)

4.3  Example: The
J2
Perturbation

As an example of the perturbed two-body problem, consider the case that
m_{b}
is a uniform sphere but
m_{a}
is some oblate earth-like body whose
J_{2}
term is the dominant gravitational perturbation such that the perturbing potential,
V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}
, in eq.(
4.1
) is then given in terms of
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
by

\displaystyle V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})\,=\,\tfrac{1}{3}\epsilon\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{r^{3}}(3\tfrac{x_{3}^{2}}{r^{2}}-1)\quad,

\displaystyle\epsilon:=\tfrac{3}{2}J_{2}R_{a}^{2}

where
R_{a}
is the equatorial radius of
m_{a}
and
x_{i}
are the elements of
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
(the cartesian components of
{\boldsymbol{r}}
in the inertial basis). As discussed in section
2.3
, there are two approaches we may take when calculating the partials
\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}
and
\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial u}
appearing in the equations of motion. One method is to first obtain the cartesian components of the perturbing force (per unit reduced mass), which is found from the above as

\displaystyle-\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\;=\;-\epsilon\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{r^{4}}\begin{pmatrix}(1-5\tfrac{x_{3}^{2}}{r^{2}})\tfrac{x_{1}}{r}\\[3.0pt]
(1-5\tfrac{x_{3}^{2}}{r^{2}})\tfrac{x_{2}}{r}\\[3.0pt]
(3-5\tfrac{x_{3}^{2}}{r^{2}})\tfrac{x_{3}}{r}\end{pmatrix}\;=\;-\epsilon{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}u^{4}\begin{pmatrix}(1-5q_{3}^{2})q_{1}\\[3.0pt]
(1-5q_{3}^{2})q_{2}\\[3.0pt]
(3-5q_{3}^{2})q_{3}\\[3.0pt]
\end{pmatrix}\,=\,-\epsilon{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}u^{4}\big((1-5q_{3}^{2}){\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}+2q_{3}\hat{{1}}_{3}\big)

where the form on the right has been expressed in terms of
{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
and simplified using
q=1
. We may then find
\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}
and
\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial u}
from the above by using eq.(
4.1
):

\displaystyle\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{u}\big[\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,-\,(\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}){\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\big]\quad,

\displaystyle\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial u}\,=\,-\tfrac{1}{u^{2}}\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}

(770)

Alternatively, we may simply use the point transformation in eq.(
4.1
),
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})=\tfrac{1}{u}\hat{{\boldsymbol{q}}}
, to express
V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}
in terms of
{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
by noting that
r=\tfrac{1}{u}
and
\tfrac{x_{i}}{r}=\tfrac{q_{i}}{q}\,
(for
i=1,2,3
). Substituting this into
V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}({\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})
given by eq.(
4.3
) leads to
V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
as

\displaystyle V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})\,=\,\tfrac{1}{3}\epsilon{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}u^{3}(3\tfrac{q_{3}^{2}}{q^{2}}-1)\,=\,\tfrac{1}{3}\epsilon{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}u^{3}(3\hat{q}_{3}^{2}-1)

Note that, as per Remark
2.3
, we do
not

simplify the above using
q=1
as, even though this would not change the numerical value of
V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}
, it would change the functional dependence on
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and thus lead to an incorrect expression for
\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}
. From the above, we find that the correct
J_{2}
perturbation terms are given by

\displaystyle\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\;=\;-2\epsilon{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}\tfrac{u^{3}}{q}\big(\hat{q}_{3}^{2}{\hat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}-\hat{q}_{3}\hat{{1}}_{3}\big)=-2\epsilon{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}u^{3}\big(q_{3}^{2}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}-q_{3}\hat{{1}}_{3}\big)

,

\displaystyle\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial u}\;=\;\epsilon{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}u^{2}(3\hat{q}_{3}^{2}-1)\,=\,\epsilon{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}u^{2}(3q_{3}^{2}-1)

where now, after differentiating, we have simplified the above using
q=1
. It can be verified that substituting eq.(
4.3
) into eq.(
770
) leads to the same result as given above.

Equations of Motion Including
J_{2}
.

eq.(
4.3
) and eq.(
4.3
) may be substituted wherever they appear in the equations of motion of section
4
.
For example, substitution into the canonical equations of motion with
s
as the independent variable — given by eq.(
4.2
) — leads to (assuming no other perturbations):

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}&\acute{q}_{i}\;=\;p_{i}\\[5.0pt]
&\acute{u}\;=\;u^{2}p_{u}\\[5.0pt]
&\acute{t}\;=\;\tfrac{1}{u^{2}}\end{array}\qquad\qquad\begin{array}[]{ll}&\acute{p}_{i}\;=\;-p^{2}q_{i}\;+\;2\epsilon{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}u(q_{3}^{2}q_{i}\;-\;1_{i3}q_{3})\\[5.0pt]
&\acute{p}_{u}\;=\;-up_{u}^{2}\;-\;\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{u^{2}}\;-\;\epsilon{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}(3q_{3}^{2}-1)\;+\;\tfrac{2}{u^{3}}p_{t}\\[5.0pt]
&\acute{p}_{t}\;=\;0\end{array}

for
i=1,2,3
and where
1_{ij}
is the Kronecker delta symbol such that the last term in the
\acute{p}_{i}
equation appears only if
i=3
.
Similarly, consider the second order equations for
\hbox to0.0pt{$\;\,\acute{\vphantom{{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}}\!\acute{\vphantom{{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}}$\hss}{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
given in eq.(
4.2.1
). After substitution of eq.(
4.3
) or eq.(
4.3
) into eq.(
4.2.1
) we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}\hbox to0.0pt{$\;\,\acute{\vphantom{q}}\!\acute{\vphantom{q}}$\hss}q_{i}\;+\;{\ell}^{2}q_{i}\;=\;2\epsilon{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}u(q_{3}^{2}q_{i}-1_{i3}q_{3})\\[5.0pt]
\hbox to0.0pt{$\;\,\acute{\vphantom{u}}\!\acute{\vphantom{u}}$\hss}u\;+\;{\ell}^{2}u\;=\;{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}\,+\,\epsilon{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}u^{2}(1-3q_{3}^{2})\end{array}

Repeating the same process with eq.(
4.2.1
) leads to

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}\accentset{\circ\circ}{q}_{i}+q_{i}\;=\;2\epsilon{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}\tfrac{u}{{\ell}^{2}}(q_{3}^{2}q_{i}+q_{3}\mathring{q}_{3}\mathring{q}_{i}\,-\,1_{i3}q_{3})\\[5.0pt]
\accentset{\circ\circ}{u}\;+\;u\;=\;\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}}\;+\;\epsilon{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}\tfrac{u}{{\ell}^{2}}\big[(1-3q_{3}^{2})u\;+\;2q_{3}\mathring{q}_{3}\mathring{u}\big]\end{array}

Note that, in eq.(
4.3
) and eq.(
4.3
), the specific angular momentum,
{\ell}=p=|\acute{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}|
, is not constant but, rather, slowly evolves according to eq.(
4.1
) which leads to

\displaystyle\acute{{\ell}}\,=\,\acute{p}\,=\,-2\epsilon{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}\tfrac{u}{{\ell}}q_{3}\acute{q}_{3}

,

\displaystyle\mathring{{\ell}}\,=\,\tfrac{\acute{{\ell}}}{{\ell}}\,=\,-2\epsilon{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}\tfrac{u}{{\ell}}q_{3}\mathring{q}_{3}

4.4  A Set of Orbit Elements for the Two-Body Problem

Equations eq.(
4.2.2
) through eq.(
4.2.2
) give the Kepler solutions for the state vectors
{\vphantom{z}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{z}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\bar{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
,
{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},\tfrac{\mathrm{d}{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}{\mathrm{d}s})
and
{\vphantom{{{\text{\textchi}}}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{{\text{\textchi}}}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},\tfrac{\mathrm{d}{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}{\mathrm{d}\tau})
— defined in eq.(
4.2.2
) —
in terms of initial conditions and the evolution parameters
s
and
\tau
.
In this section, we will return our attention to the perturbed two-body problem and use the initial conditions of the Kepler solution as a set of orbit elements. We will use variation of parameters to find the equations of motion for these elements when some perturbing force (per unit reduced mass),
{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}
, is present.

We will use the constants
{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}=({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}},u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}},{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}},w_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}})
— which are initial condition for the Kepler solution given by eq.(
4.2.2
) — as our set of orbit elements where
{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
is the canonical coordinates,
{\vphantom{z}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{z}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
, with
p_{u}
replaced by
w=u^{2}p_{u}
:

\displaystyle{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;=\;({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},u,{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},w)\quad,

\displaystyle w\,=\,u^{2}p_{u}\,=\,\acute{u}\,=\,-\dot{r}

(809)

We choose to use
w
rather than
p_{u}
primarily because the Kepler solution for
w
has simpler algebraic form compared to the solution for
p_{u}
(as seen by eq.(
4.2.2
)). We choose
{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
rather than
{\vphantom{{{\text{\textchi}}}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{{\text{\textchi}}}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
because, as discussed in Remark
4.2
, the latter does not fully define the state and thus neither will
{\vphantom{{{\text{\textchi}}}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{{\text{\textchi}}}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
.

The perturbed equations of motion for
\acute{{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}
with
s
as the evolution parameter are given by eq.(
4.2
), along with eq.(
4.2.1
). However, recall from section
4.2.2
that the Kepler solutions with
\tau
as the evolution parameter have unit natural frequency whereas the solutions with
s
as the evolution parameter have a natural frequency of
{\ell}=p
which is
not
constant for perturbed motion. Therefore, in the following developments, we will use
\tau
as the evolution parameter when deriving the equations of motion for the elements,
{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
, as this will considerably expedite the variation of parameters procedure. As such, to avoid undue confusion of the evolution parameter, it may be best at this point to dissociate
w
from
\tfrac{\mathrm{d}u}{\mathrm{d}s}
and simply think of it as a new variable defined as
w=u^{2}p_{u}
.

4.4.1  Variation of Parameters Using Keplerian Initial Conditions

Since we will use
\tau
as the evolution parameter, we will need the equations of motion for
\mathring{{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}
. eq.(
4.2
) gives
\mathring{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}
,
\mathring{u}
, and
\mathring{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}
. The remaining equation for
\mathring{w}
can be found from differentiating the definition of
w
in eq.(
809
):

\displaystyle\mathring{w}=\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}\acute{u}=\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}s}u=\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}\hbox to0.0pt{$\;\,\acute{\vphantom{u}}\!\acute{\vphantom{u}}$\hss}u

or,

\displaystyle\mathring{w}=\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}(u^{2}p_{u})=2u\mathring{u}p_{u}+u^{2}\mathring{p}_{u}

(810)

after substitution of
\hbox to0.0pt{$\;\,\acute{\vphantom{u}}\!\acute{\vphantom{u}}$\hss}u
from eq.(
4.2.1
), or
\mathring{p}_{u}
from eq.(
4.2
), the perturbed equations of motion for
\mathring{{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}
are given by

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{llll}\mathring{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}&\qquad,&\qquad\quad\mathring{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,-{\ell}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,+\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}u^{2}}{\vphantom{f}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{f}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\[6.0pt]
\mathring{u}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}w&\qquad,&\qquad\quad\mathring{w}\,=\,-{\ell}u+\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}}\,+\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}f_{u}({\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}})\end{array}

where
{\ell}=p
and where the perturbation terms,
{\vphantom{f}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{f}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
f_{u}
, are given in terms of the total perturbing force by eq.(
3.2
):

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lll}{\vphantom{f}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{f}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}&\,=\,-\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}+{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{u}\big[{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}-({\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}){\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\big]\\[10.0pt]
f_{u}&\,=\,-\tfrac{\uppartial V^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}}{\uppartial u}\,+\,\alpha_{u}\,=\,-\tfrac{1}{u^{2}}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\end{array}

The Unperturbed (Kepler) Solution Form.

For the unperturbed Kepler problem, the above terms vanish and the closed-form solution is then given in terms of initial conditions and
\tau
by eq.(
4.2.2
) with
{\ell}s=\tau
:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{cc}{\text{\footnotesize{Kepler solution,}}}\\
{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,=\,{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}},\tau)\end{array}\;\;\left\{\;\;\begin{array}[]{rl}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{\tau}&\,=\,{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos\tau\,+\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\sin\tau\\[6.0pt]
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{\tau}&\,=\,-{\ell}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\sin\tau\,+\,{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos\tau\\[6.0pt]
u_{\tau}&\,=\,(u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}})\cos\tau\,+\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}w_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\sin{\tau}\,+\,\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}}\\[6.0pt]
w_{\tau}&\,=\,-{\ell}(u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}})\sin\tau\,+\,w_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos\tau\end{array}\right.

where
{\ell}=p
is constant for Keplerian motion. Note that, as per Remark
4.3
, the above implicitly assumes
\tau_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}=0
but that
\tau
can more generally be taken as
\Delta\tau=\tau-\tau_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
for arbitrary
\tau_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
. From eq.(
4.2.2
), we see that the Kepler solution for
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
in terms of
{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
and
\tau
is given by:

\displaystyle{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{\tau}\;=\;\tfrac{1}{u_{\tau}}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{\tau}

\displaystyle\,=\,\frac{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos\tau\,+\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\sin\tau}{(u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}})\cos\tau\,+\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}w_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\sin{\tau}\,+\,\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}}}

\displaystyle{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{\tau}\;=\;u_{\tau}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{\tau}-w_{\tau}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{\tau}

\displaystyle\,=\,{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\,+\,\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}}\big[{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}(\cos\tau-1)\,-\,{\ell}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\sin{\tau}\big]

\displaystyle\,=\,{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\,+\,\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}}\big({\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{\tau}-{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\big)

where
{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}=u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-w_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
.

The Perturbed Solution Form.

For the perturbed problem, we seek a solution that has the
same algebraic form
as the unperturbed solution in eq.(
4.4.1
). In order to do this, we no longer treat
{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
as a set of constants/initial conditions but, instead, as a set of slowly evolving/osculating elements,
{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}(\tau)
:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{cc}{{\text{\footnotesize{Kepler Solution,}}}}\\[3.0pt]
{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,=\,{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}},\tau)\\[5.0pt]
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,=\,{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}},\tau)\\[3.0pt]
{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,=\,{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}},\tau)\end{array}

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{cc}{\text{\footnotesize{Perturbed Solution,}}}\\[3.0pt]
{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,=\,{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\big({\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}(\tau),\tau\big)\\[5.0pt]
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,=\,{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\big({\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}(\tau),\tau\big)\\[3.0pt]
{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,=\,{\vphantom{v}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{v}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\big({\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}(\tau),\tau\big)\end{array}

where the algebraic form of the Kepler solutions and perturbed solutions are the same and is given by eq.(
4.4.1
) and eq.(
4.4.1
). For the perturbed solution, the osculating elements will have some governing equations of motion,
\mathring{{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
, which vanish in the unperturbed case. These equations are derived later on.

\,\scriptscriptstyle{\blacksquare}\,
Remark 4.4
.

—For perturbed motion, we can no longer choose to express solutions in terms of either
s
or
{\ell}
as we could for the Kepler problem;
{\ell}=p
is no longer constant and eq.(
4.2.2
) and eq.(
752
) no longer hold:

\displaystyle\tau\,\neq\,{\ell}s\hskip 18.49988pt,\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988ptf(s)\,\neq\,\left.{f(\tau)}\right|_{\tau={\ell}s}

(842)

Note that
{\ell}
appears on the right-hand-side of the equations in eq.(
4.4.1
). We could treat
{\ell}
as an additional element with
\mathring{{\ell}}
governed by eq.(
4.3
). However, this is not necessary as we may use the simple relation
{\ell}(\tau)=p(\tau)
.

Interpretation the Elements,
{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}(\tau)
.

We have
required
the perturbed solution to have the algebraic form seen in eq.(
4.4.1
) and, in so doing, have abandoned the interpretation of
{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
as actual initial conditions. Instead, the meaning of
{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
is seen by inverting eq.(
4.4.1
), which leads to
43
43

43

eq.(
4.4.1
) is equivalent to eq.(
758
).

\displaystyle{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}={\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\big({\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}(\tau),\tau\big)\;\;\left\{\;\;\;\begin{array}[]{lll}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}(\tau)\,=\,{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\cos\tau-\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\sin\tau\\[6.0pt]
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}(\tau)\,=\,{\ell}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\sin\tau+{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\cos\tau\\[6.0pt]
u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}(\tau)\,=\,(u-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}})\cos\tau-\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}w\sin\tau+\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}}\\[6.0pt]
w_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}(\tau)\,=\,{\ell}(u-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}})\sin\tau+w\cos\tau\end{array}\right.

Only for unperturbed Keplerian motion are the above
{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
accurately interpreted as initial conditions.
For general perturbed motion,
{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
is now interpreted as a new set of “slow” coordinates that are
defined
in terms of the “fast” coordinates,
{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
, by the above relations.
From these relations, along with eq.(
3.3
), or eq.(
4.1
), we see that,
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
,
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
, and
{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
satisfy the same orthogonal relations as
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
,
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
, and
{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\;=\;{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;=\;1\\[4.0pt]
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\;=\;{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;=\;{\ell}^{2}\\[4.0pt]
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\;=\;{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;=\;0\end{array}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\times{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\;=\;{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\times{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\;=\;{\hat{{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\\[4.0pt]
{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\times{\hat{{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\,=\,{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\\[4.0pt]
{\hat{{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\times{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\,=\,{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\end{array}

we also see that

\displaystyle\cos\tau\;=\;{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\;=\;{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}

,

\displaystyle\sin\tau\;=\;{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\;=\;-{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}

(857)

To see the meaning of
{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
, let us again consider the Laplace-Runge-Lenz (LRL) vector/eccentricity vector,
{\boldsymbol{e}}
, the Hamilton vector,
{\boldsymbol{k}}
, and the specific angular momentum vector,
\bm{\ell}
, discussed in eq.(
4.1
):

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{e}}=\tfrac{1}{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}(\dot{{\boldsymbol{r}}}\times\bm{\ell}-{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{3.44444pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}{\mkern 1.5mu\hat{\mkern-1.5mu{\boldsymbol{r}}}})\quad,

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{k}}=\tfrac{1}{{\ell}^{2}}(\bm{\ell}\times{\boldsymbol{e}})\quad,

\displaystyle\bm{\ell}={\boldsymbol{r}}\times\dot{{\boldsymbol{r}}}

The above are all conserved for Kepler motion and their normalization gives an orthonormal basis,
\{{\mkern 1.5mu\hat{\mkern-1.5mu{\boldsymbol{e}}}},{\mkern 1.5mu\hat{\mkern-1.5mu{\boldsymbol{k}}}},\hat{\bm{\ell}}\}
, often called the
perifocal basis
(which is inertial/stationary for Keperian motion and osculating for perturbed motion).
{\mkern 1.5mu\hat{\mkern-1.5mu{\boldsymbol{e}}}}
and
{\mkern 1.5mu\hat{\mkern-1.5mu{\boldsymbol{k}}}}
defining the orbital plane;
{\mkern 1.5mu\hat{\mkern-1.5mu{\boldsymbol{e}}}}
directed towards periapse,
{\mkern 1.5mu\hat{\mkern-1.5mu{\boldsymbol{k}}}}
directed along the velocity at periapse (direction tangent to the orbit), and
\hat{\bm{\ell}}
directed normal to the orbit plane, completing the right-handed triad.

\,\scriptscriptstyle{\blacksquare}\,
Remark 4.5
.

If
\tau_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
is chosen to coincide with periapse, then
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
is a unit vector directed along the LRL/eccentricity vector, and
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
is directed along the Hamilton vector (with magnitude
p_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}={\ell}
).
Then, just as
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
define the instantaneous LVLH basis (as discussed in Remark
3.3
), the elements
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
and
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
define the
osculating
perifocal basis:
44
44

44

More specifically,
\{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}},{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}},{\hat{{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\}
are actually the
components
of the osculating perifocal basis vectors in the inertial basis.

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{cc}\text{osculating}\\
\text{perifocal basis}\end{array}\left\{\hskip 9.24994pt\begin{array}[]{cll}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}&\;=\;{\hat{{\vphantom{e}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{e}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\;=\;\text{unit eccentricity vector}\\[5.0pt]
{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}&\;=\;{\hat{{\vphantom{k}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{k}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\;=\;\text{unit Hamilton vector}\\[5.0pt]
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\times{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}&\;=\;{\hat{{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\;=\;\text{orbit normal unit vector}\end{array}\right.

Note nothing
requires

{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
and
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
to coincide with periapse; we could instead choose initial conditions such that

\{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}},{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}},{\hat{{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}={\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\times{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\}
is a “snap shot” of the LVLH basis at any convenient choice of
\tau_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
.

Equations of Motion for the Elements
{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
.

To obtain the governing equations of motion for the elements,
{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
, we use the fact that the Kepler solution form — given by eq.(
4.4.1
) — but with
{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}(\tau)
no longer constant, must satisfy the
perturbed
equations of motion for
{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
given by eq.(
4.4.1
).
Differentiating eq.(
4.4.1
) with respect to
\tau
— and treating
{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
as
{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}(\tau)
— leads to

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rl}\mathring{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}&=\big[-{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\sin\tau+\tfrac{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}}{{\ell}}\cos\tau\big]+\mathring{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos\tau+\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}(\mathring{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\tfrac{\mathring{{\ell}}}{{\ell}})\sin\tau\\[3.0pt]
&=\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}+\mathring{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos\tau+\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}(\mathring{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\tfrac{\mathring{{\ell}}}{{\ell}})\sin\tau\\[10.0pt]
\mathring{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}&=-\big[{\ell}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos\tau+{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\sin\tau\big]-({\ell}\mathring{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}+\mathring{{\ell}}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}})\sin\tau+\mathring{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos\tau\\[3.0pt]
&=-{\ell}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}-({\ell}\mathring{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}+\mathring{{\ell}}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}})\sin\tau+\mathring{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos\tau\\[10.0pt]
\mathring{u}&=\big[-(u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}})\sin\tau+\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}w_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos{\tau}\big]+(\mathring{u}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}+\tfrac{2{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{3}}\mathring{{\ell}})\cos\tau+\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}(\mathring{w}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\tfrac{\mathring{{\ell}}}{{\ell}}w_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}})\sin\tau-\tfrac{2{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{3}}\mathring{{\ell}}\\[3.0pt]
&=\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}w+(\mathring{u}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}+\tfrac{2{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{3}}\mathring{{\ell}})\cos\tau+\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}(\mathring{w}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\tfrac{\mathring{{\ell}}}{{\ell}}w_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}})\sin\tau-\tfrac{2{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{3}}\mathring{{\ell}}\\[10.0pt]
\mathring{w}&=-\big[{\ell}(u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}})\cos\tau+w_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\sin\tau\big]-{\ell}(\mathring{u}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}+\tfrac{2{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{3}}\mathring{{\ell}})\sin\tau-\mathring{{\ell}}(u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}})\sin\tau+\mathring{w}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos\tau\\[3.0pt]
&=-{\ell}(u-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}})-{\ell}(\mathring{u}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}+\tfrac{2{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{3}}\mathring{{\ell}})\sin\tau-\mathring{{\ell}}(u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}})\sin\tau+\mathring{w}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos\tau\end{array}

where the second equality for each of the above follows from the requirement that the algebraic relations in eq.(
4.4.1
) must still hold.
Equating the above to the perturbed equations of motion, we find

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lll}\mathring{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}=\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}&\;\;\rightarrow&\qquad\mathring{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos\tau+\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}(\mathring{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\tfrac{\mathring{{\ell}}}{{\ell}})\sin\tau={\vphantom{0}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{0}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\[4.0pt]
\mathring{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}+{\ell}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=\tfrac{{\vphantom{f}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{f}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{{\ell}u^{2}}&\;\;\rightarrow&\qquad-({\ell}\mathring{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}+\mathring{{\ell}}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}})\sin\tau+\mathring{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos\tau\;=\;\tfrac{{\vphantom{f}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{f}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{{\ell}u^{2}}\\[4.0pt]
\mathring{u}=\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}w&\;\;\rightarrow&\qquad(\mathring{u}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}+\tfrac{2{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{3}}\mathring{{\ell}})\cos\tau+\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}(\mathring{w}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\tfrac{\mathring{{\ell}}}{{\ell}}w_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}})\sin\tau-\tfrac{2{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{3}}\mathring{{\ell}}=0\end{array}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lll}\mathring{w}+{\ell}u-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}}=\tfrac{f_{u}}{{\ell}}&\;\;\rightarrow&\qquad-{\ell}(\mathring{u}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}+\tfrac{2{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{3}}\mathring{{\ell}})\sin\tau-\mathring{{\ell}}(u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}})\sin\tau+\mathring{w}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\cos\tau=\tfrac{f_{u}}{{\ell}}\end{array}

The first two relations may be solved for
\mathring{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
and
\mathring{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
, and the last two relations for
\mathring{u}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
and
\mathring{w}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
. The result, which may be expressed in a variety of ways, is

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rlll}\mathring{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}&\,=\,-\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}(\tfrac{1}{{\ell}u^{2}}{\vphantom{f}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{f}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}-\tfrac{\mathring{{\ell}}}{{\ell}}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})\sin\tau\;=\;-\tfrac{\sin\tau}{{\ell}^{2}u^{2}}\big({\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}-{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\otimes{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\big){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{f}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{f}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\[10.0pt]
\mathring{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}&\,=\,(\tfrac{1}{{\ell}u^{2}}{\vphantom{f}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{f}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}-\tfrac{\mathring{{\ell}}}{{\ell}}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})\cos\tau\,+\,\tfrac{\mathring{{\ell}}}{{\ell}}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\;=\;\tfrac{1}{{\ell}u^{2}}\Big(\big[{\vphantom{f}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{f}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}-({\vphantom{f}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{f}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}){\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\big]\cos\tau\,+\,({\vphantom{f}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{f}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}){\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\Big)\\[10.0pt]
\mathring{u}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}&\,=\,-\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}(\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}f_{u}\,-\,\tfrac{\mathring{{\ell}}}{{\ell}}w)\sin\tau-\tfrac{2{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{3}}\mathring{{\ell}}(1-\cos\tau)\\[6.0pt]
&\,=\,-\tfrac{1}{{\ell}^{2}u^{2}}\Big(\big[u^{2}f_{u}\,-\,({\vphantom{f}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{f}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})\tfrac{w}{{\ell}}\big]\sin\tau\,+\,\tfrac{2{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}}({\vphantom{f}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{f}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})(1-\cos\tau)\Big)\\[10.0pt]
\mathring{w}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}&\,=\,(\tfrac{1}{{\ell}}f_{u}-\tfrac{\mathring{{\ell}}}{{\ell}}w)\cos\tau\,+\,\tfrac{\mathring{{\ell}}}{{\ell}}w_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\,+\,\tfrac{2{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}}\mathring{{\ell}}\sin\tau\\[6.0pt]
