&\,=\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}u^{2}}\Big(\big[u^{2}f_{u}-({\vphantom{f}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{f}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})\tfrac{w}{{\ell}}\big]\cos\tau\,+\,({\vphantom{f}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{f}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})(\tfrac{w_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}}{{\ell}}\,+\,\tfrac{2{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}}\sin\tau)\Big)\end{array}

where the second equalities come from substitution of
\mathring{{\ell}}
given by

\displaystyle\mathring{{\ell}}\,=\,\mathring{p}\,=\,\mathring{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}u^{2}}{\vphantom{f}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{f}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}

(915)

After substituting
{\vphantom{f}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{f}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and

from eq.(

) into eq.(

, we obtain the governing equations of motion for the elements in terms of the total perturbing force (per unit reduce mass),

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathring{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}&\,=\,-\tfrac{\sin\tau}{{\ell}^{2}u^{3}}\big({\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}-{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\otimes{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}-{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\otimes{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\big){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}\\[10.0pt]
\mathring{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}&\,=\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}u^{3}}\Big(({\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}-{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\otimes{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}-{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\otimes{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})\cos\tau\;+\;{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\otimes{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\Big){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}\\[10.0pt]
\mathring{u}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}&\,=\,\tfrac{1}{{\ell}^{2}u^{3}}\Big((u{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}+\tfrac{w}{{\ell}}{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})\sin\tau\,-\,\tfrac{2{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}}(1-\cos\tau){\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\Big){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}\\[10.0pt]
\mathring{w}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}&\;=\;-\tfrac{1}{{\ell}u^{3}}\Big((u{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}+\tfrac{w}{{\ell}}{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})\cos\tau\,-\,(\tfrac{w_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}}{{\ell}}+\tfrac{2{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.41112pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}}\sin\tau){\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\big){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}\end{array}

where
{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}=\tfrac{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}}{p_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}}
and
{\ell}=p=p_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
. The above equations are written mostly in terms of
{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
but can be expressed solely in terms of the elements,
{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
, using the relations given in eq.(

) and eq.(
4.4.1
). For instance, we may express the above equations for
\mathring{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
and
\mathring{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
using

\displaystyle{\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}-{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\otimes{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}-{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\otimes{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\;\;=\;\;{\hat{{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\otimes{\hat{{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\;\;=\;\;{\text{1}}\hskip-0.56905pt_{3}-{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\otimes{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}-{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\otimes{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}

,

\displaystyle{\hat{{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}={\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\times{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}={\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}\times{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}

(922)

\displaystyle u{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}+\tfrac{w}{{\ell}}{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\,=\,u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}+\tfrac{w_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}}{{\ell}}{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}+\tfrac{{\mkern-4.0mu\textnormal{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\raisebox{2.16998pt}{\scriptsize{--}}\cr$\mkern 4.0mu{\mathrm{k}}$\cr}}}\vphantom{k}}}{{\ell}^{2}}({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}-{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}})

which follows from the fact that
\{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\hat{{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\}
are the LVLH basis vectors (or, rather, their components in the inertial basis) along with eq.(
4.4.1
). The above equations verify that

\displaystyle\mathring{{\ell}}=\mathring{p}=\mathring{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}=\mathring{p}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}=\mathring{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}

(925)

Note that if the perturbing force is also some central force of any form (i.e., if
{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}=a_{1}{\hat{{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}=a_{1}{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
), then eq.(
4.4.1
) leads to
\mathring{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}=\mathring{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}={\vphantom{0}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{0}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and these elements remain constant. Similarly, if the perturbing force is only in the orbit normal direction (i.e., if
{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}=a^{\scalebox{0.7}{$1$}}{\hat{{\vphantom{{\ell}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{\ell}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
), then
\mathring{u}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}=\mathring{w}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}=0
and these elements remain constant.

An Alternative Set of Orbit Elements.

Given that the preceding developments used
\tau
as the evolution parameter, one may feel inclined to use the coordinates
{\vphantom{{{\text{\textchi}}}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{{\text{\textchi}}}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},\tfrac{\mathrm{d}{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}{\mathrm{d}\tau})=({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},u,{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},\tilde{w})
— where
{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}=\mathring{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}
and
\tilde{w}=\tfrac{u^{2}}{{\ell}}p_{u}=\mathring{u}
— rather than the coordinates
{\vphantom{\upxi}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\upxi}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=({\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},\tfrac{\mathrm{d}{\bar{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}{\mathrm{d}s})=({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},u,{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},w)
that we used above.
Doing so would change little about the above developments; we would simply replace
w
and
w_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
with
w={\ell}\tilde{w}
and
w_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}={\ell}\tilde{w}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
and replace
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
with
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}={\ell}{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
and
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}={\ell}{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
, in eq.(
4.4.1
) and eq.(
4.4.1
). The equations of motion would simply be
\mathring{{\hat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}=\accentset{\circ\circ}{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}
and
\mathring{\tilde{w}}=\accentset{\circ\circ}{u}
given in eq.(
4.2.1
). After going through the same procedure for variation of parameters to find
\mathring{{\vphantom{{{\text{\textchi}}}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{{\text{\textchi}}}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
, the result differs only slightly from eq.(
4.4.1
):

The elements
{\vphantom{{{\text{\textchi}}}}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{{{\text{\textchi}}}}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
and variational equations of motion,

The above equations for

\tilde{w}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}}
and use
({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}},u_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}},{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}},\tilde{w}_{{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}})
as a set of orbit elements.

5.  CONCLUSION

This work has explored the intersection of classical Hamiltonian mechanics and the regularization of central force dynamics through projective decompositions. By developing a new regularizing transformation in an analytical mechanics framework, we have presented a systematic approach to linearizing central force motion in a redundant-dimensional phase space, offering theoretical and computational tools for studying orbital dynamics.

Our approach began with a coordinate-based derivation of a new regularizing transformation, illustrating how the introduction of redundant dimensions and a redefined evolution parameter effectively linearizes the governing equations for a certain class of radial force fields. Through explicit closed-form solutions for inverse square and inverse cubic radial force potentials, we demonstrated the robustness of this technique, leading to the formulation of a new set of orbital elements tailored to Kepler (or Coulomb) dynamics. These elements, along with their associated variational equations, provide a novel perspective on perturbed two-body motion, facilitating new computationally efficient treatments of gravitational (and electrostatic) systems. Numerical validation incorporating the
J_{2}
gravitational perturbation further confirmed the efficacy of this framework.

There are several avenues for future research. Within the framework presented here, the next step on the classical Hamiltonian path would be the application of Hamilton-Jacobi theory to obtain action-angle coordinates and their subsequent use in perturbation theory (analogous to the Lie series methods of Brouwer, Deprit, Hori). Furthermore, the symmetries, reduction, and integrability of the transformed Hamiltonian system warrant further investigation than what was presented here. Additionally, extending this regularization framework to more intricate systems, such as multi-particle systems (e.g., the three-body problem) or those arising in non-classical mechanical contexts (e.g., incorporating relativistic or quantum effects), presents an interesting challenge.

In summary, this work has contributed a novel perspective on the regularization of mechanical Hamiltonian systems. We have provided both a practical computational tool and a deeper theoretical understanding of Hamiltonian methods for transforming and regularizing central force Hamiltonian systems.

Appendix A
HAMILTON’S EQUATIONS WITH NON-CONSERVATIVE FORCES

We will outline the process — within the classic analytical dynamics framework — for including non-conservative forces in Hamilton’s canonical equations of motion, and for transforming such forces between different coordinate sets.

Generalized Forces for Arbitrary Canonical Coordinates,
({\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})
.

Suppose some non-conservative forces,
{\textbf{{{a}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}
, acts on the particle, or system of particles, of interest. In this work,
{\textbf{{{a}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}
is usually the force per unit reduced mass.
For some arbitrary canonical/symplectic coordinates,
({\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})
, Hamilton’s canonical equations of motion including
{\textbf{{{a}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}

are given by

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lll}&\dot{{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial{{K}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\;-\;{\vphantom{A}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{A}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{\scalebox{0.7}{$Q$}}&\hskip 9.24994pt,\\[9.0pt]
&\dot{{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,-\tfrac{\uppartial{{K}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\;+\;{\vphantom{A}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{A}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{\scalebox{0.7}{$P$}}&\hskip 9.24994pt,\end{array}\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\begin{array}[]{ll}&{\vphantom{A}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{A}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{\scalebox{0.7}{$Q$}}\;:=\;\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}\\[8.0pt]
&{\vphantom{A}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{A}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{\scalebox{0.7}{$P$}}\;:=\;\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}\end{array}

where
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}
are the cartesian components of
r
and
{\textbf{{{a}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}
in an inertial basis.
Note that it is often the case that
{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
are
true configuration-level coordinates (e.g., cartesian position coordinates, spherical coordinates, Euler angles, etc.) and
{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
are velocity-level coordinates such that
{\textbf{{{r}}}}={\textbf{{{r}}}}({\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},t)
is not a function of
{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
. When this is the case, the above simplifies to

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lll}&\dot{{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial{{K}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}&\\[9.0pt]
&\dot{{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,-\tfrac{\uppartial{{K}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\;+\;{\vphantom{A}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{A}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}&\hskip 9.24994pt,\end{array}\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\begin{array}[]{ll}&\\[8.0pt]
&{\vphantom{A}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{A}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;:=\;\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}\end{array}

Where we have dropped the subscript on the generalized forces, which now appear only in the dynamics for the momenta.
Although various derivations of eq.(
A
) and eq.(
A
) can be found in many texts on analytical mechanics
[
32
,
14
,
30
]
, we briefly summarize the process below.

Derivation of eq.(
A
).

We may account for the effects of some non-conservative force on the equations of motion by adding the work due to this force, denoted
W^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}
, to the action such that Hamilton’s principle leads to

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rlllll}0\,=\,\delta I&\,=\,\delta\int_{t_{\scalebox{0.45}{${\boldsymbol{\circ}}$}}}^{t_{f}}({\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\dot{{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}-{{K}}+W^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}})\,{\mathrm{d}}t\,=\,\int_{t_{\scalebox{0.45}{${\boldsymbol{\circ}}$}}}^{t_{f}}(\dot{{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\delta{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,+\,{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\delta\dot{{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,-\,\delta{{K}}\,+\,\delta W^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}})\,{\mathrm{d}}t\\[5.0pt]
&\,=\,\int_{t_{\scalebox{0.45}{${\boldsymbol{\circ}}$}}}^{t_{f}}(\dot{{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\delta{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,-\,\dot{{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\delta{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,-\,\delta{{K}}\,+\,\delta W^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}})\,{\mathrm{d}}t\\[5.0pt]
&\,=\,\int_{t_{\scalebox{0.45}{${\boldsymbol{\circ}}$}}}^{t_{f}}\big((\dot{{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}-\tfrac{\uppartial{{K}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\delta{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,+\,(-\dot{{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}-\tfrac{\uppartial{{K}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\delta{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,+\,{\textbf{{{a}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\delta{\textbf{{{r}}}}\big){\mathrm{d}}t\,=\,0\end{array}

where we have used
\delta W^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}={\textbf{{{a}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\delta{\textbf{{{r}}}}
as well as fact that the variations vanish at the boundaries:
\left.{\delta{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\right|_{t_{\scalebox{0.45}{${\boldsymbol{\circ}}$}}}\!\!=\left.{\delta{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\right|_{t_{\scalebox{0.45}{${\boldsymbol{\circ}}$}}}\!\!={\vphantom{0}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{0}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
,
\delta t_{\scalebox{0.45}{${\boldsymbol{\circ}}$}}=0
, and likewise at
t_{f}
.
Integration by parts was used to replace
{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\delta\dot{{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}
with
-\dot{{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\delta{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
inside the integral.
Next, it is often assumed that
{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
are true configuration/position-level coordinates and that the momenta,
{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
, are velocity-level coordinates such that
{\textbf{{{r}}}}={\textbf{{{r}}}}({\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},t)
is a function only of the configuration coordinates and, possibly, time.
Yet, this is not the most general case as a canonical transformation can render this assumption inaccurate.
46
46

46

Take the Delaunay variables for example. These are canonical coordinates for orbital motion for which
r
is a function of both the configuration and momentum coordinates.

Thus, taking the most general case that
{\textbf{{{r}}}}={\textbf{{{r}}}}({\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},t)
, we find that
\delta W^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}
is given by

\displaystyle\delta W^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}\,=\,{\textbf{{{a}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\delta{\textbf{{{r}}}}\,=\,{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}(\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\delta{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,+\,\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\delta{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})\,=\,(\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\delta{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,+\,(\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\delta{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}

(943)

Such that eq.(
A
) becomes

\displaystyle\delta I\,=\,\int_{t_{\scalebox{0.45}{${\boldsymbol{\circ}}$}}}^{t_{f}}\Big((\dot{{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}-\tfrac{\uppartial{K}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}+\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\delta{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\;-\;(\dot{{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}+\tfrac{\uppartial{K}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}-\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\delta{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\Big){\mathrm{d}}t=0

(944)

for the above to hold for all arbitrary
\delta{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
\delta{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
requires that their coefficients be zero. This leads to the canonical equations of motion given by eq.(
A
).

\,\scriptscriptstyle{\blacksquare}\,
Remark A.1

(computing the generalized forces)
.

In computing the generalized forces in eq.(
A
),
{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}
are the cartesian components of the vector
{\textbf{{{a}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}
in an inertial basis and and

{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}={\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},t)
are the cartesian components of the position vector,
r
, in the same basis, expressed in terms of the canonical coordinates and, possibly, time. However, the generalized forces can more generally be computed from the vectors
r
and
{\textbf{{{a}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}
without the need to specify any basis:

\displaystyle A_{\scalebox{0.7}{$Q_{i}$}}\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial{\textbf{{{r}}}}}{\uppartial P_{i}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\textbf{{{a}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial\dot{{\textbf{{{r}}}}}}{\uppartial\dot{P}_{i}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\textbf{{{a}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}\hskip 18.49988pt,

\displaystyle A_{\scalebox{0.7}{$P_{i}$}}\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial{\textbf{{{r}}}}}{\uppartial Q_{i}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\textbf{{{a}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial\dot{{\textbf{{{r}}}}}}{\uppartial\dot{Q}_{i}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\textbf{{{a}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}

(945)

Transformation of the Generalized Forces Under a Canonical Transformation.

Now suppose that we perform some general canonical transformation from
({\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})
to new canonical coordinates,
({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})
.
47
47

47

The developments of this section apply the same weather
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
are minimal or non-minimal canonical coordinates.

Since
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
are also canonical coordinates, the derivation of the equations of motion including non-conservative forces exactly parallels eq.(
A
)-eq.(
944
). In general, we assume
{\textbf{{{r}}}}={\textbf{{{r}}}}({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},t)
such that
\delta W^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}
is now given by eq.(
943
) with
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
taking the place of
{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
, respectively.
The requirement that
\delta I=0
then leads to canonical equations of motion which are precisely the same
form
as eq.(
A
):

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lll}&\dot{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\;-\;{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{q}&\hskip 9.24994pt,\\[9.0pt]
&\dot{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,-\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\;+\;{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{p}&\hskip 9.24994pt,\end{array}\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\begin{array}[]{ll}&{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{q}\;:=\;\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}\\[8.0pt]
&{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{p}\;:=\;\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}\end{array}

where
{{H}}
is the Hamiltonian for the
({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})
phase space.
What we would like to know is, given some canonical transformation between
({\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})
and
({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})
, what then is the relation between the above
{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and the
{\vphantom{A}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{A}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
defined in eq.(
A
)? We will present the most general case as well a more specific, yet common, case.

•

Case 1.
Consider the most general case:

–

({\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})
are arbitrary canonical/symplectic coordinates such that, in general,
{\textbf{{{r}}}}={\textbf{{{r}}}}({\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},t)
.

–

({\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})
and
({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})
are related by a general canonical transformation such that, in general,
{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}={\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},t)
and
{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}={\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},t)
.

Then the relation between
{\vphantom{A}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{A}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
can be found from eq.(
A
) as

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lll}{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{q}\,:=\,\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}\,=\,\big(\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}+\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\big)^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}&\,=\,\big(\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}+\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}\big)\\[10.0pt]
{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{p}\,:=\,\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}\,=\,\big(\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}+\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\big)^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}&\,=\,\big(\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}+\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}\big)\end{array}

From the above, along with the definitions of
{\vphantom{A}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{A}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{\scalebox{0.7}{$Q$}}
and
{\vphantom{A}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{A}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{\scalebox{0.7}{$P$}}
in eq.(
A
), eq.(
A
) then gives the equations of motion and generalized forces as

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lll}&\dot{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\;-\;{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{q}&\hskip 9.24994pt,\\[9.0pt]
&\dot{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,-\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\;+\;{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{p}&\hskip 9.24994pt,\end{array}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{q}\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{A}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{A}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{\scalebox{0.7}{$P$}}+\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{A}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{A}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{\scalebox{0.7}{$Q$}}\\[8.0pt]
{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{p}\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{A}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{A}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{\scalebox{0.7}{$P$}}+\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{A}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{A}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{\scalebox{0.7}{$Q$}}\end{array}

Thus, once one knows the relation between the phase space coordinates, one may then use the above equations on the right to calculate the relation between the generalized forces.

Case 2.
Now consider a more specific, common, case:

–

({\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})
are configuration-level and velocity-level coordinates, respectively, such that
{\textbf{{{r}}}}={\textbf{{{r}}}}({\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},t)
is
not
a function of
{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}

–

({\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})
and
({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})
are related by a time-dependent
point
transformation such that
{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}={\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},t)
and
{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}={\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},t)
.

The first of the above leads to
{\vphantom{A}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{A}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{Q}={\vphantom{0}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{0}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
such that the equations of motion for
{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
are given by eq.(
A
).
The second condition — that the two phase spaces are related by a canonical
point
transformation — means that
\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}=O
.
eq.(
•
‣
A
) then simplifies to

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lll}&\dot{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}&\\[9.0pt]
&\dot{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,-\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\;+\;{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}&\hskip 9.24994pt,\end{array}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}&\\[8.0pt]
&{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,\equiv\,{\vphantom{\alphaup}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alphaup}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{p}\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\vphantom{A}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{A}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\end{array}

Note that if
{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{P}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{P}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
are simply the position and velocity components in an inertial basis (if
{\vphantom{Q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{Q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}={\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
), then
{\vphantom{A}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{A}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}={\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}
is simply the components of the force from non-conservative forces in that same basis.

Appendix B
EXTENDED PHASE SPACE (COORDINATE-BASED VIEW)

Notation
.

In the following,
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=(r_{1},\dots,r_{\scriptscriptstyle{N}})
denote some inertial cartesian coordinates, and
({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})=(q_{1},\dots,q_{n},p_{1},\dots,p_{n})
denote some generalized position/configuration and momenta coordinates (where
n\leq N
is the degrees of freedom, that is, dimension of configuration space). A “hat”,
\,\widehat{\raisebox{0.96873pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\bullet}$}}\,
, does
not
denote a normalized unit vector or unit tuple but will, instead, denote objects associated with extended space.

Suppose we wish to use some evolution parameter,
{\varepsilon}
, that is related to the time through some specified differential relation:

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}t}{\mathrm{d}{\varepsilon}}\,=\,\mathring{t}\hskip 18.49988pt,\hskip 18.49988pt{\mathrm{d}}t\,=\,\mathring{t}{\mathrm{d}}{\varepsilon}

(960)

where
\mathring{(\,)}
will denote differentiation with respect to
{\varepsilon}
and where, in general,
\mathring{t}
may be some function of the generalized coordinates,
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
, the generalized velocities,
\dot{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}
, and time,
t
. Now, the classic action integral written in terms of the Lagrangian is given by
I=\int_{t_{\scalebox{0.45}{${\boldsymbol{\circ}}$}}}^{t_{f}}{{L}}{\mathrm{d}}t
. Using the above differential relation, this same integral with
{\varepsilon}
as the evolution parameter is given by
I=\int_{{\varepsilon}_{\scalebox{0.45}{${\boldsymbol{\circ}}$}}}^{{\varepsilon}_{f}}{{L}}\mathring{t}{\mathrm{d}}{\varepsilon}
. We then define extended generalize coordinate and velocity vectors, and the
extended Lagrangian
,
\widehat{{L}}
, as

\displaystyle{\widehat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}:=({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},t)\hskip 18.49988pt,\hskip 18.49988pt\mathring{{\widehat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}:=(\mathring{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}},\mathring{t})\hskip 18.49988pt,\hskip 18.49988pt\widehat{{L}}({\widehat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},\mathring{{\widehat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}})\,:=\,\mathring{t}{{L}}({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},\dot{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}},t)

(961)

Where the configuration space has increased by one dimension to include the time as another generalized coordinate with generalized velocity
\mathring{t}
.

The Extended Phase space.

Now, the conjugate momenta coordinate are classically defined as
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=\tfrac{\uppartial{{L}}}{\uppartial\dot{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
. In an analogous manner, we now define the momenta conjugate to the time as

\displaystyle p_{t}\,:=\,\tfrac{\uppartial\widehat{{L}}}{\uppartial\mathring{t}}\,=\,{{L}}\,-\,{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\dot{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,-{{H}}

(962)

where the equality
\tfrac{\uppartial\widehat{{L}}}{\uppartial\mathring{t}}={{L}}-{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\dot{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}
is derived in
the footnote
48
48

48

From
\widehat{{L}}:=\mathring{t}{{L}}
, then
\tfrac{\uppartial\widehat{{L}}}{\uppartial\mathring{t}}={{L}}+\mathring{t}\tfrac{\uppartial{{L}}}{\uppartial\mathring{t}}
. Noting that
{{L}}({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},\dot{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}},t)
only depends on
\mathring{t}
through the relation
\dot{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}=\tfrac{1}{\mathring{t}}\mathring{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}
, it follows that
\tfrac{\uppartial{{L}}}{\uppartial\mathring{t}}=\tfrac{\uppartial{{L}}}{\uppartial\dot{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\tfrac{\uppartial\dot{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}{\uppartial\mathring{t}}=-{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\tfrac{\mathring{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}{\mathring{t}^{2}}=-\tfrac{1}{\mathring{t}}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\dot{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}
. Thus,
\tfrac{\uppartial\widehat{{L}}}{\uppartial\mathring{t}}={{L}}+\mathring{t}\tfrac{\uppartial{{L}}}{\uppartial\mathring{t}}={{L}}-{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\dot{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}
, which is precisely the classical definition of
-{{H}}
.
.
From the above, we see that
p_{t}
is equal to the
negative
of the classic Hamiltonian,
{{H}}
, where
{{H}}
itself is often (but not always) equal to the total energy.
We then define the
extended Hamiltonian
,
\widehat{{H}}({\widehat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\widehat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
— where
({\widehat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\widehat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})
are extended coordinate and momenta vectors for the extended phase space — using the a Legendre transformation in the usual manner:

\displaystyle{\widehat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}:=\,({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},t)\hskip 18.49988pt,\hskip 18.49988pt{\widehat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}:=\,({\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},p_{t})\hskip 18.49988pt,\hskip 18.49988pt\widehat{{H}}({\widehat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}},{\widehat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}})\,:=\,{\widehat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\mathring{{\widehat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\,-\,\widehat{{L}}\,=\,\mathring{t}({{H}}\,+\,p_{t})\,=\,0

(963)

where
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
are the usual coordinates and momenta for the non-extended phase space.
Note from the above that the value of
\widehat{{H}}
is always equal to zero on account of eq.(
962
). Then — as we will soon show — the canonical equations of motion with
{\varepsilon}
as the evolution parameter are obtained from the extended Hamiltonian as

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lllll}\mathring{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial\widehat{{H}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,-\,\mathring{t}{\vphantom{\alpha}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alpha}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{q}\\[5.0pt]
\mathring{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,-\tfrac{\uppartial\widehat{{H}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,+\,\mathring{t}{\vphantom{\alpha}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alpha}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{p}\end{array}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{llll}\mathring{t}\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial\widehat{{H}}}{\uppartial p_{t}}\\[5.0pt]
\mathring{p}_{t}\,=\,-\tfrac{\uppartial\widehat{{H}}}{\uppartial t}\,+\,\mathring{t}\alpha_{t}\end{array}

where
{\vphantom{\alpha}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alpha}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
are the generalized forces defined in section
A
and where we have also defined an additional generalized “force”,
\alpha_{t}
(which really has units of power). If
{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{N}}
denotes the components of the nonconservative force vector in the inertial
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
-basis, then these generalized forces for arbitrary
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
are given by:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{ll}{\vphantom{\alpha}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alpha}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{q}=\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}\\[5.0pt]
{\vphantom{\alpha}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alpha}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{p}=\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle{\textsf{{T}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}\\[5.0pt]
\alpha_{t}\,:=\,-({\vphantom{\alpha}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alpha}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{p}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\dot{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}+{\vphantom{\alpha}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alpha}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{q}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\dot{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}})\,=\,-{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}(\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}-\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}})\,=\,-{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}(\dot{{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}-{\rotatebox[origin]{10.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{t}{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}})\end{array}

For the case that
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
are true coordinate-level and velocity-level variables, respectively — such that
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}={\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},t)
is
not
a function of the momenta — then the canonical equations of motion in the extended phase space are given by

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lllll}&\hskip 9.24994pt\mathring{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial\widehat{{H}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\\[5.0pt]
&\hskip 9.24994pt\mathring{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,-\tfrac{\uppartial\widehat{{H}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,+\,\mathring{t}{\vphantom{\alpha}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alpha}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\\[8.0pt]
{\text{\footnotesize{where:}}}&\hskip 9.24994pt{\vphantom{\alpha}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alpha}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,=\,{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\end{array}

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{llll}\mathring{t}\,=\,\tfrac{\uppartial\widehat{{H}}}{\uppartial p_{t}}\\[5.0pt]
\mathring{p}_{t}\,=\,-\tfrac{\uppartial\widehat{{H}}}{\uppartial t}\,+\,\mathring{t}\alpha_{t}\\[8.0pt]
\alpha_{t}\,=\,-{\vphantom{\alpha}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alpha}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\dot{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,-{\vphantom{\alpha}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alpha}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\tfrac{\uppartial{{H}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\end{array}

From eq.(
962
), it is seen that
p_{t}=-{{H}}
. In this way, the extended phase space is itself a sort of non-minimal phase space where
p_{t}
is related to the other canonical variables through
p_{t}=-{{H}}
. However, this should only be treated as a relationship between the
value
of
p_{t}
and the
value
of
{{H}}
.
{{H}}
is a function of
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
,
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
, and
t
.
p_{t}
is not. In the extended phase space,
p_{t}
is treated as an independent momenta variable just as
t
is treated as an independent generalized coordinate. However,
after
obtaining the equations of motion, the relation
p_{t}=-{{H}}
can be substituted into the equations of motion themselves (although this is not necessary).

Derivation of eq.(
B
).

Hamilton’s principle, including the work from non-conservative forces,
W^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}
, is given in the extended phase space by

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rl}0\,=\,\delta I\,=\,\delta\int_{{\varepsilon}_{\scalebox{0.45}{${\boldsymbol{\circ}}$}}}^{{\varepsilon}_{f}}({\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\dot{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}-{{H}}+W^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}})\mathring{t}\,{\mathrm{d}}{\varepsilon}&\,=\,\delta\int_{{\varepsilon}_{\scalebox{0.45}{${\boldsymbol{\circ}}$}}}^{{\varepsilon}_{f}}({\widehat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\mathring{{\widehat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}-\widehat{{H}}+\mathring{t}W^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}})\,{\mathrm{d}}{\varepsilon}\\[8.0pt]
&\,=\,\int_{{\varepsilon}_{\scalebox{0.45}{${\boldsymbol{\circ}}$}}}^{{\varepsilon}_{f}}\big(\delta{\widehat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\mathring{{\widehat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}-\mathring{{\widehat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\delta{\widehat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}-\delta\widehat{{H}}+\delta(\mathring{t}W^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}})\big)\,{\mathrm{d}}{\varepsilon}\end{array}

where we have used integration by parts along with the fact that the variations vanish at the boundaries. Using the same process, the term involving
W^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}
leads to

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{lll}\int_{{\varepsilon}_{\scalebox{0.45}{${\boldsymbol{\circ}}$}}}^{{\varepsilon}_{f}}\delta(\mathring{t}W^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}){\mathrm{d}}{\varepsilon}\,=\,\int_{{\varepsilon}_{\scalebox{0.45}{${\boldsymbol{\circ}}$}}}^{{\varepsilon}_{f}}(\mathring{t}\delta W^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}-\mathring{W}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}\delta t){\mathrm{d}}{\varepsilon}\,=\,\int_{{\varepsilon}_{\scalebox{0.45}{${\boldsymbol{\circ}}$}}}^{{\varepsilon}_{f}}\mathring{t}(\delta W^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}-\dot{W}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}\delta t){\mathrm{d}}{\varepsilon}\end{array}

where we have used
\mathring{(\;)}=\mathring{t}\dot{(\;)}
.
Let us take
{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
and
{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
to be arbitrary canonical variables such that, in general,
{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}={\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},t)
.
With
t
now treated as any other generalized coordinate,
\dot{W}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}
and
\delta W^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}
are now given
by
49
49

49

Note in eq.(
B
) that
{\rotatebox[origin]{10.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{t}{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}
is now included in
\delta W^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}
but that
\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial p_{t}}=0
.

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rlll}\dot{W}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}\,=\,{\textbf{{{a}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\accentset{\mbox{\scriptsize{.}}}{{\textbf{{{r}}}}}\,=\,{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\dot{{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,=\,{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\big(\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\widehat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\dot{{\widehat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}+\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\widehat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\dot{{\widehat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\big)&\,=\,{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\big(\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\dot{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}+{\rotatebox[origin]{10.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{t}{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}+\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\dot{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\big)\\[6.0pt]
\delta W^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}\,=\,{\textbf{{{a}}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\delta{\textbf{{{r}}}}\,=\,{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\delta{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,=\,{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\big(\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\widehat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\delta{\widehat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}+\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\widehat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}}\delta{\widehat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}\big)&\,=\,{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\big(\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\delta{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}+{\rotatebox[origin]{10.0}{$\partial$}\mkern-2.0mu}_{t}{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\delta t+\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\delta{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\big)\end{array}

The term in parenthesis in eq.(
B
) is then given by

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rl}\delta W^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}-\dot{W}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}\delta t\;\,=\,\;{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\delta{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,-\,{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\accentset{\mbox{\scriptsize{.}}}{{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\delta t&\,=\,{\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\big(\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\delta{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}+\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\delta{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,-\,(\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\dot{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}+\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\dot{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}})\delta t\big)\\[5.0pt]
&\,=\,{\vphantom{\alpha}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alpha}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{p}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\delta{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,+\,{\vphantom{\alpha}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alpha}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{q}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\delta{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,-\,({\vphantom{\alpha}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alpha}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{p}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\dot{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,+\,{\vphantom{\alpha}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alpha}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{q}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\dot{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}})\delta t\end{array}

where we have used
{\vphantom{\alpha}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alpha}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{p}:={\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}
and
{\vphantom{\alpha}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alpha}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{q}:={\vphantom{a}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{a}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathrm{nc}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\tfrac{\uppartial{\vphantom{r}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{r}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}
.
Substitution of eq.(
B
) and eq.(
B
) into eq.(
B
) then leads to

\displaystyle\delta I\,=\int_{{\varepsilon}_{\scalebox{0.45}{${\boldsymbol{\circ}}$}}}^{{\varepsilon}_{f}}\Bigg\{(\dot{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}-\tfrac{\uppartial\widehat{{H}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}+\mathring{t}{\vphantom{\alpha}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alpha}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{q}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\delta{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,+\,(-\dot{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}-\tfrac{\uppartial\widehat{{H}}}{\uppartial{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}+\mathring{t}{\vphantom{\alpha}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alpha}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{p}){\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\delta{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\,+\,(\mathring{t}-\tfrac{\uppartial\widehat{{H}}}{\uppartial p_{t}})\delta p_{t}\,+\,\big[-\mathring{p}_{t}-\tfrac{\uppartial\widehat{{H}}}{\uppartial t}-\mathring{t}({\vphantom{\alpha}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alpha}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{p}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\dot{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}\,+\,{\vphantom{\alpha}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{\alpha}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}_{q}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}\dot{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}})\big]\delta t\Bigg\}\,{\mathrm{d}}{\varepsilon}\,=\,0

(984)

In order for the above to hold for all arbitrary
\delta{\widehat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
and
\delta{\widehat{{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}
, their coefficients must vanish. This leads to the canonical equations of motion with respect to the evolution parameter
{\varepsilon}
as seen in eq.(
B
) with the generalized forces defined in eq.(
B
).

Appendix C
NUMERICAL VERIFICATION

The following are run including the
J_{2}
perturbation, with initial conditions given in terms of classic orbit elements
(a,e,i,\omega,\Omega,f\equiv\tau)
by
a_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=8.59767038{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}10^{3}({\mathrm{km}})
,
e_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=0.2
,
i_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=20({\mathrm{deg.}})
,
\omega_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=70({\mathrm{deg.}})
,
\Omega_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=135({\mathrm{deg.}})
, and
f_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}\equiv\tau_{{\scalebox{0.7}{$0$}}}=0
.

Figure 1:
Verifying accuracy of the canonical equations of motion for
({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},u,{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},p_{u})
, showing errors in the classic orbit elements (COEs), as compared to propagating the modified equinoctial elements (MEEs). (
J_{2}
included).

Figure 2:
Verification that
q={\lvert{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\rvert}=1
and
{\hat{{\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}}}{\mkern 1.5mu\cdot\mkern 1.5mu}{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}=0
are “integrals of motion” and that
p={\lvert{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}}\rvert}={\ell}
is equal to the (specific) angular momentum magnitude. (
J_{2}
included).

Figure 3:
Verification that the STM for
({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},u,{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},p_{u})
, with
t
as the evolution parameter, is a symplectic matrix (
J_{2}
included).

Figure 4:
Verification that the STM for
({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},u,{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},p_{u})
, with
s
as the evolution parameter, is
not
a symplectic matrix (
J_{2}
included).

Figure 5:
Verification that the STM for
({\vphantom{q}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{q}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},u,{\vphantom{p}{\underline{\smash{\mathrm{p}}\mkern-1.0mu}{\mkern 1.0mu}}},p_{u},t,p_{t})
, with
s
as the evolution parameter, is again a symplectic matrix (
J_{2}
included).

Figure 6:
Verification of the variational equations for the new elements, showing cartesian ECI position and velocity errors as compared to propagating the modified equinoctial elements (MEEs). (
J_{2}
included).

Figure 7:
Mean rates, estimated by a simple least squares fit, of new elements compared to mean rates of the modified equinoctial elements (MEEs). Y-axes in dimensionless units. (
J_{2}
included).
